Plant These 7 Flower Seeds in November for Gorgeous Early Blooms Next Year

Sorry Squirrels, This Simple Trick Stops Pests Eating Tulip Bulbs (and it Beats Chicken Wire Every Time)

What Your Dahlias Need Most After the First Frost Hits – 5 Ways to Save Your Favorite Blooms for Next Year

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Tulip fire can make your eagerly anticipated spring bulbs look distorted and spotty. But these quick bulb planting checks can help you to prevent calamity later

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Planting some tulips is one of the most enjoyable ways to pass some time this month. It offers the promise of vivid, saturated color to punctuate the winter months. Too often, however, the anticipation can turn to heartbreak, as early leaves emerge stunted, twisted, and scarred with spots. This is classic tulip fire: an aggressive fungal blight that leaves tulips looking as though they were scorched by an open flame.

The frustrating reality of tulip fire is that while symptoms appear in spring, the infection usually begins months earlier during tulip planting. The pathogen enters unnoticed on dry bulb scales, or lies dormant in beds where infected tulips grew before. Once established, no post-planting drench or spray can save infected stems. Your best defense is prevention before your tulip bulbs go in the ground. A few careful checks at planting time give next year's tulips a much better shot. Just a few proactive measures this fall will ensure that your tulips emerge clean, robust, and full of vibrant blooms. Here’s how to address the perils of tulip fire before it ever has a chance to cause problems – so you can look forward to spring with confidence.

Tulip fire is a common tulip disease caused by the fungal pathogen Botrytis tulipae. While closely related to common gray mold (B. cinerea), which attacks soft fruits and perennials, B. tulipae is host-specific and targets tulips almost exclusively. This host specificity is an advantage for gardeners. If you deny the fungus its preferred host for 3-4 seasons, resting populations in the soil gradually starve and decline.

Recognizing early symptoms is key to containment. In early spring, infected bulbs send up stunted, crumpled shoots known as fireheads. These sickly stems become blanketed in fuzzy gray fungal spores that blow or splash onto neighboring plants during cool, rainy weather. Tiny, water-soaked flecks quickly spread across foliage, coalescing into scorched brown blotches, while flower buds rot before opening. Wet spring conditions across USDA hardiness zones 3-8 accelerate spore dispersal, and can turn an isolated infection into an outbreak within a week.

Most cultivated tulip hybrids are at risk in damp, overcrowded conditions. However, species tulips with lower profiles (like Tulipa clusiana or T. tarda) and sturdy Darwin hybrid varieties demonstrate better resilience than dense, double-flowered late types. If you’re still deciding which bulbs to get, try bold Red Darwin Hybrid Tulip Bulbs from Amazon for weather resistant stems, and dreamily elegant Salmon Impression Darwin Hybrid Tulip Bulbs from Plant Addicts for natural vigor. Here's how to prevent your tulips turning into fireheads. Grab your bulbs and get ready!

Preventing tulip fire adds only a few minutes to your fall planting routine, but the benefits are immeasurable. Proactive defense focuses on basic cultural care: thoughtful checks, site and timing prep, and ample airflow. The following simple checks take just a little extra preparation, providing peace of mind and protecting your spring displays long before the first green shoots pierce the soil…

Before planting your bulbs, empty your bulb bags onto an outdoor potting bench or table, and inspect each one individually. A healthy, viable tulip bulb should feel solid, firm, and dense, with an intact papery outer tunic. Gently press the base plate and tip with your thumb. Any that give under your thumb, show mold, feel spongy, or have sunken yellow-brown patches should go in the trash.

Sign up for the Gardening Know How newsletter today and receive a free copy of our e-book "How to Grow Delicious Tomatoes".

Next, peel back a section of the papery tunic. Look carefully for sclerotia: tiny, hard, black or dark-brown resting bodies that resemble crushed pepper or poppy seeds. To help get a better look up close, the Jarlink 30X 60X Illuminated Jewelers Loupe, available from Amazon is equipped with bright LED illumination to check for those black sclerotia specks, as well as hidden scale lesions. These small sclerotia carry the tulip fire fungus between seasons. If you detect black specks or lesions, bag the bulb and throw it in the trash. Never toss diseased stock into home compost piles.

Resist the urge to plant your tulips while the ground is still warm. Botrytis tulipae fungal spores and resting sclerotia thrive in mild, damp soil conditions between 50°F and 65°F (10-18°C). Planting prematurely exposes bulbs to active soil-borne pathogens before the bulbs can establish robust roots. Delay your tulip planting until mid-to-late November or early December across most regions, after several hard overnight frosts have chilled the ground.

Soil temperatures should consistently measure below 50°F (10°C) at 6 inches (15cm). Cold soil suppresses fungal activity and forces dormant spores into inactivity. Planting after the first hard frosts is one of the most effective defenses against tulip fire. Even if an early frost hardens the surface, the soil beneath remains workable. Using a long-handled bulb planter like the Jardineer Bulb Transplanter from The Home Depot works well even in frost-encrusted fall soils.

Soil hygiene and thoughtful rotation are critical defenses against tulip fire. If your border suffered from tulip fire last spring, millions of sclerotia will have been shed into the earth as the leaves and flower petals decomposed. These resting bodies can survive in loam for 3-5 years, waiting to infect any new tulips that push up through the contaminated soil, and start the cycle all over again. Give that bed at least 3 years without tulips, and plant new bulbs somewhere that's never had the disease. Fill affected borders with non-host species that tulip fire cannot infect, such as daffodils (narcissus), alliums, grape hyacinths (muscari), or crocuses.

If bed space is tight, containers are an easy workaround. Transition your tulips into patio containers. However, always dump out old potting soil, sanitize containers with a dilute vinegar or mild bleach wash, and plant in fresh, sterile soilless potting mix enriched with perlite. A lovely blend is Espoma Organic Potting Mix, available from Walmart, which is free of garden pathogens, and gives a low-release nutrient boost.

A bulb that sits in soggy ground all winter is in trouble, whether tulip fire shows up or not. Good drainage is critical. Tulip bulbs thrive in moisture, but can rot quickly in stagnant water. Planting in heavy, compacted clay or low-lying depressions also creates damp conditions where fungal spores multiply rapidly. Check your planting site a day or so after a soaking rain. Water that is still standing there, or dirt that balls up and sticks to your boots, means the spot would drain too slowly for tulips.

Select a planting site situated on high ground or a gentle slope. If you garden on heavy clay, avoid digging individual narrow holes. Instead, excavate the whole planting area 8-10 inches (20-30cm) deep. Blend some horticultural grit or coarse sand into the soil to help with drainage. Alternatively, for persistently soggy gardens, skip the fight and grow your tulips in containers or raised beds. You can get small bags of best-selling Miracle-Gro Perlite from The Home Depot for just a few dollars, and sprinkle a few handfuls into the planting site (or use it in your containers!).

Crowded planting designs may look lush in magazine spreads, but tightly packed bulbs create stagnant, humid conditions. After a few years, offsets and daughter bulbs fill in a bit more each season, and after a while the leaves will crowd each other. If tulips are jammed together, leaves that touch will stay wet longer after rain or a heavy dew, and spores won't have far to go to get from one plant to the next.

Give your tulips room to breathe by spacing bulbs 4-6 inches (10-15cm) apart in all directions. Make sure the planting site is not jammed against a fence or the side of the house. Choose open, sunny sites with steady airflow. Use jute twine like Vivifying Natural Jute from Amazon looped around some wooden stakes to help mark your plantings. Also, if you are naturalizing tulips in mixed borders, lift and divide dense perennial clumps every few years before planting your bulbs.

Old tulip leaves don't just vanish when they're left on the soil. On plants that were sick, fresh sclerotia form on the dying tissue and drop into the bed, and they can stay there until tulips come up through that ground again. Infected leaves, decaying stem bases, and dropped flower petals all produce fresh sclerotia as they wither. Left untouched, it decomposes into the topsoil, recharging the fungal spore bank.

Before planting new bulbs, do a fall cleanup. Rake through your beds and clear out old leaves, spent stems, and decayed organic matter. Pull back mulch to inspect the soil, discarding any dried plant remnants and flower parts from the previous growing season. Make sure you bag these remnants instead of composting them. You can buy best-selling Fabulas Yard Waste Bags from Walmart in triple packs.

In spring, you need to stay proactive. Deadhead any spent flowers off suspect plants before their petals drop onto lower foliage, and clear out leaves and stems from anything that shows symptoms. Dig up any twisted firehead shoots, including the bulbs and adjacent soil. Do this the moment symptoms appear to keep airborne spores from spreading. Cleanup won't necessarily promise a clean bed, but it ensures there's a lot less fungus left behind to start the next round.

Once tulip fire takes hold in an established bed, it is one of the most persistent and frustrating bulb diseases to eradicate. So this fall care routine is massively beneficial in helping to break the fungal life cycle before it can cause damage. Take the right steps now when planting, and then you just have to wait for those first green shoots and a vibrant, spotless tulip display in spring.

Need more ideas for getting the most from your plants, indoors and out, and looking for the best seasonal expert advice delivered straight to your inbox? Sign up for the free Gardening Know How Newsletter!

With a microbiology background focused on fungi, he’s spent over a decade solving tough and intricate gardening problems. After spinal injuries and brain surgery, Tyler’s approach to gardening changed. It became less about the hobby and more about recovery and adapting to physical limits. Core Scientific plans to shut down Bitcoin mining despite Q3 revenue hitting $233M

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Core Scientific, once the largest Bitcoin [BTC] miner, has released its Q3 2026 report, but at the same time it has also announced its plans to completely exit Bitcoin mining by the end of 2026.

During its Q3 2026 earnings call, Chief Financial Officer Jim Nygaard said the company expects to complete the mining wind-down in the fourth quarter and enter 2027 as a high-density colocation provider.

This decision came as the firm’s Q3 results were primarily driven by the expansion of its high-density colocation (HDC) business, which supports AI and high-performance computing (HPC) workloads.

Remarking on the same, CEO Adam Sullivan said,

We are nearing full delivery under our first colocation agreement while construction under our second is underway across five campuses.

According to the reports, the company’s colocation revenue surged nearly 60% quarter-over-quarter to $215.4 million, compared to $136.7 million in Q2 2026 and $15 million in Q3 2025.

In fact, its total revenue also increased to $232.9 million, up from $164.2 million in the previous quarter and $81.1 million a year earlier.

As of the 30th of September, Core Scientific was billing for 485 megawatts (MW) of capacity, up from 395 MW in the previous quarter, representing more than $700 million in average annualized colocation revenue.

That said, the company also reported net income of $1.178 billion, compared to a net loss of $1.15 billion in Q2 2026 and $144 million in Q3 2025.

Meanwhile, adjusted EBITDA more than tripled quarter-over-quarter to $133.6 million, with its margin expanding to 57% from 25% in the previous quarter. Core Scientific’s gross profit also climbed to $123.3 million, compared to $70 million in Q2.

However, on the mining side, self-mining revenue declined to $12.3 million from $21.5 million in the previous quarter, while hosted mining revenue stood at $5.17 million. At the same time, revenue from hosting equipment for other Bitcoin miners also declined to $5.2 million from $8.7 million.

For those unaware, Core Scientific began its AI infrastructure transition in 2024 through hosting agreements with CoreWeave, covering 590 MW of capacity, of which 485 MW was billable by September 2026. However, the transition also comes with challenges.

Core Scientific recorded $266.5 million in mining-related asset impairments in Q1 2026 amid weaker Bitcoin prices, low mining profitability, and declining equipment values. It also recorded a $41.9 million loss after terminating mining equipment agreements with Block and Proto in Q2.

That said, Core Scientific is not the only one, as, according to AMBCrypto’s recent report, Bitcoin miners are increasingly selling BTC to fund operations while shifting their infrastructure towards AI and HPC.

This comes as miners swung from a huge inflow spike of about +7K BTC  in early 2026 to an outflow of about -5.7K BTC in early November, while the price slid from around $95K to $75.5K.

However, the latest netflow has settled back to a small positive reading of about 179.4 BTC, which suggests the miner selling pressure has cooled.

Win at Everton would put Coventry on right side of history in survival fight

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

None of the 21 sides who had 12 points after 10 matches, over the past decade, were relegated from the Premier League

If Coventry win at Everton on Friday night they will take an enormous stride towards maintaining their Premier League status. That might seem a huge statement with 28 games remaining, but victory would put Frank Lampard’s side on the right side of recent history. For now, they stand on a precipice.

Coventry have taken nine points from nine league matches. Eight teams in the previous 10 seasons were in the same position – seven stayed up. The exception, Leicester in 2024-25, were newly promoted and have tumbled downwards ever since.

There were 21 clubs in the last decade with 12 points after 10 matches. None of them went down and eight finished in the top half. Lampard’s first return to a former club this season suddenly looks remarkably important. Even if a win cannot guarantee Coventry’s top-flight survival, they would occupy what has been a strong position historically.

Lampard has a similar managerial record to former teammate (and new West Ham manager) Scott Parker, a midfielder alongside whom he made 27 appearances for Chelsea. Excellent in the second tier - Lampard reached the playoffs at worst in each of his three Championship campaigns - he has never fully convinced in the Premier League. Given he took Chelsea to fourth place and the FA Cup final in 2019-20, perhaps that is unfair. Equally, they had finished third the year before and were champions in two of the four campaigns prior to that.

Though he kept Everton up in 2022, Lampard won only nine of his 38 league matches with the club. No Everton manager with at least as many games has a lower win percentage in all competitions, with that post followed by a caretaker spell back at Chelsea in which he won only once in 11 matches.

Regardless of Lampard’s ability, what can we reasonably expect from Coventry? Capology estimate the club have the lowest wage bill in the division, while Transfermarkt rate their squad as having the third-lowest market value. This challenging reality was reflected in pre-season predictions. Opta gave Coventry a better chance of staying up than their fellow promoted sides, Hull and Ipswich. The former have delivered the weakest performance of the three and are now deemed the most likely of the trio to go back down. They have faced all the current top three, in fairness, while Hull haven’t played any of them and Ipswich have only tackled one.

Coventry remain third-favourites for the drop, as they were in pre-season. It is just the teams below them that have changed, with Sunderland and Crystal Palace now propping up the forecast. With the possibility of Manchester City facing a points deduction, third bottom might be enough to remain in the Premier League.

That issue notwithstanding, Coventry’s underlying performance needs to improve. Not that anyone would have cared when they scored three times to beat Sunderland last weekend when only recording 0.69 in expected goals. Newcastle had the better of the chances when losing 4-1 at the Coventry Building Society Arena, too. Their match against Hull is the only one this season in which they were on the right side of the xG.

Coventry led the Championship for expected goal difference last season. Lampard’s Chelsea improved on this metric in 2019-20 from the season before and were only 0.6 behind champions Liverpool. The 48-year-old has shown he can lead teams to strong statistical performance, though Coventry are clearly far more of an underdog this season than Lampard’s sides were in his strongest campaigns.

Everton did not beat any of the top seven at the Hill Dickinson Stadium last season and lost 2-0 to Chelsea in their previous home league match. They have generally performed well against struggling sides, though. Their only home defeats to bottom-half sides last season were against Newcastle and Tottenham, two clubs who were in the Champions League.

Victory on Friday is a tall order for Coventry, but if Lampard can win at Everton for the first time in his managerial career, his side will be well set for the task of staying up.

One More Round of Winter Container Inspiration

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Hi everyone! Before we jump in, a quick note: We had a little technical hiccup today, and our GPOD didn’t publish when it was supposed to. If you missed it, you can catch up on Ellen Goldfarb and Tracy Buttrick’s winter container designs here.

Now, let’s finish out the week with one more round of winter inspiration from Ellen and Tracy of WhatsBlooming! I hope these containers have given you plenty of ideas to play around with in your own seasonal displays.

This cedar-shake square pot in Tenafly, New Jersey, is one of a pair flanking the steps of this rustic porch. It is under the roof, so the artificial cardinals won’t disintegrate! For height, we used birch logs, some short stems of curly willow, and lots of silver bell eucalyptus.

The greens were desert juniper (Juniperus osteosperma, Zones 3–9), noble fir (Abies procera, Zones 5–6), and incense cedar (Calocedrus decurrens, Zones 5–8). Pinecones, both frosted and brown, and small bunches of silver brunia finished off the planter.

Close-up of a tall, stand-alone pot out in front of a house in Allendale, New Jersey. It can be seen from all sides. We used tall birch logs and tall stems of heavily berried Chinese juniper (Juniperus chinensis, Zones 4–9). Silver bell eucalyptus, small curly willow branches, frosted pinecones, silver brunia clusters, and incense cedar branches fill out the arrangement.

The owner of this house in Saddle River just loved red winterberries (Ilex verticillata, Zones 3–9), so we used lots of them! There was plenty of room for height, so we added tall curly willow, which contrasted nicely with the white background.

There is some tall Chinese juniper tucked in and rosettes of magnolia leaves with natural brown pinecones, silver brunia clusters, a little noble fir, and lots of incense cedar around the rim.

Here you can see the previous planter on the front porch with its partner.

Thanks so much to Ellen and Tracy for sharing this incredible collection of winter containers with us! I hope you continue to submit images of all the beautiful containers you create. I can only imagine what spring and summer look like!

Hopefully everyone found a new combination, material, or idea to try in their own containers this winter. Have a wonderful weekend, and we’ll see you back here on Monday!

Evercade Nexus review: Banjo-Kazooie and Doom make this retro handheld a must-have

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Explore projects, trends and inspiring design work.

Discover design software, tools and creative resources.

Start exploring exclusive deals, expert advice and more.

Packed with digital art tutorials, inspiration and expert advice from ImagineFX.

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The Evercade Nexus is the best version of what Evercade has been building towards. It’s comfortable, well-made, and easy to use, and it comes with a big screen, twin sticks, and the full cartridge library from day one. Banjo-Kazooie in the box shows what it can do in 3D, while the 8-bit and 16-bit classics that built the brand are still right at home. An easy recommendation.

Big, bright screen that makes 3D and mid-90s pixel art look crisp

Banjo-Kazooie bundled in the box, with native widescreen, 60fps and Stop ’n’ Swop content

Plays the entire existing Evercade games library

EverSync lets a second Nexus play a game from just one cartridge

Why you can trust Creative Bloq Our expert reviewers spend hours testing and comparing products and services so you can choose the best for you. Find out more about how we test.

Controls: D-pad, face buttons, twin analogue sticks, bumpers and triggers

Connectivity: Wi-Fi 6, Bluetooth audio, 3.5mm

Evercade Nexus is the retro game brand cracking its knuckles, stretching and embracing an even larger catalogue of retro games. Evercade has established itself as the easy entry point to retro gaming. It’s the antidote to wasting afternoons lost to getting ROMs running and fiddling with settings, and as much as I love ways to play like the Sinclair Gamer Card, I also enjoy the ease of Evercade’s cart approach, and I’ve been a fan since the first Evercade EXP.

With the Nexus, we have a retro game console that can play all the existing properly licensed games in the Evercade library, with authentic emulation and physical cartridges that scratch the collector itch, while ushering us into a ‘new’ 3D era. You pop a cart in, and you play.

So this is 'next gen', then, in the most Evercade way, with new cartridge collections stretching up to around 1998, proper 3D alongside the pixels, and a handheld that looks and feels like it belongs in 2026. I got my first hands-on with the console at the Nexus community launch in September, where I got to play Doom and Banjo-Kazooie, and I also got context for Nexus, as the room was lined with old Evercade consoles leading up to this moment. You can find many of these in our best retro consoles guide.

First thing to know: the Nexus is much bigger than the Evercade EXP and the newer EXP-R and can feel a little chunky compared to the older model. The Nexus is a proper handheld in the vein of the Nintendo Switch, not a jacket-pocket gadget. That said, the Nexus is still smaller and lighter than a Nintendo Switch 2; for context, it’s about the size of the Switch 2 display, and I've been happy playing for hours at a time.

The design has some fun touches you expect from a Sony console. The rear grips feature a subtle mesh of raised 'A' symbols that nod to the Evercade logo, and the textured rubber pads sit where your hands land to keep it from shifting. For me, and even though I love it, the Switch 2 can feel slippery in handheld mode, as its slimmer design and smaller, curved finger bridges can be too subtle. The Nexus doesn't move in the hand; it’s bulkier, yes, but also more solid in the grip.

Up front is the 5.89-inch IPS display, with controls arranged around it as you expect from a modern handheld. Evercade has opted for symmetrical analogue sticks rather than the Xbox and Nintendo offset layout, and I'm a fan. It feels closer to a PlayStation pad, and it suits 3D games well while also making 2D games easy to play.

Under the display, you get a quad-core 1.5GHz processor, 512MB of RAM, and 4GB of storage, plus Wi-Fi 6 for system updates. The 5,000mAh battery lasts more than five hours and charges via USB-C. You also get Bluetooth headphone support, a 3.5mm jack and front-facing stereo speakers.

Sign up to Creative Bloq's daily newsletter, which brings you the latest news and inspiration from the worlds of art, design and technology.

The D-pad, you see, is also symmetrically placed opposite the face buttons, but it’s also a big step up from the older EXP's pad. It's light and responsive, which is exactly what you want for fighting games, and I've sunk plenty of time into King of the Fighters 2002 on it, and it's still going well. Switching to the classic controls for the new Atari collections feels natural, too.

The buttons are a touch clicky and light; they don't have the premium feel of pricier handhelds. Next to a Switch 2, you can see where the money went. At this price, and for the games it’s designed to play, from Doom to Activision Decathlon, that's fair, and the controls never distract.

The screen is the Nexus's biggest upgrade and one reason for its size, because embracing 3D and the more complex pixel art of games from the mid-90s onward demands a larger screen. It's a 5.89-inch IPS panel at 840 x 512, rated at over 500 nits. It isn't OLED, so don't expect the deep blacks of a premium handheld, but I came away impressed. Colours pop, contrast is solid and older games look crisp without losing their grit. The simulated CRT and other retro game effects on many cartridges sit nicely, too.

Banjo-Kazooie comes bundled in the box, and it's a brilliant showcase. Banjo-Kazooie and Banjo-Tooie both get new native ports with widescreen support, higher resolution and 60fps, and that second analogue stick transforms how they play. These aren't old N64 ROMs run through an emulator. They've been nicely reworked, with modern control options and Stop 'n' Swop content. It's one of the best reasons to buy the Nexus and joins the likes of the recent Croc 2 remaster in showing how these old 3D platfomers can be improved on new hardware..

The display keeps up with faster games, too. I've been flipping between the new DOOM and DOOM II collection and the SNK titles on the new Neo Geo Arcade 4 cartridge I always drift back to because Metal Slug 4 is just too good, and the Nexus handles both. Twin sticks make DOOM feel far more natural than a D-pad ever could, and placing them symmetrically works wonders.

TATE mode is back as well, rotating the screen for vertical arcade games. The Nexus's larger size makes this mode easier to use, as I often found the Evercade EXP a little too narrow to be comfortable. The Nexus's bulk makes it a tad better than the Switch 2 for TATE mode, as I found when playing the Toaplan Arcade Garage: Flying Fire Shark; Nintendo’s console can be too slim for its own good.

The Nexus is only half the story, of course, because Evercade's physical games are the main draw, and the new handheld plays the entire existing library: more than 80 cartridges and over 700 officially licensed games from Atari, Capcom, Taito, SNK, Rare, Activision and more. Each collection arrives in its own box with a full-colour manual, and building a shelf of them is a joy a digital storefront can't match.

No downloads, no games vanishing from a subscription service, no hunting for ROMs in dodgy corners of the internet. Cart in, game on. It's refreshing and plays into the idea of celebrating how we play and not just what we play, and by plan or by accident. Evercade has become the zeitgeist brand, treating gaming like a vinyl collection.

The Nexus also debuts EverSync, a local wireless multiplayer feature. Only one player needs the cartridge; the second console receives the game from the first. It's clever, and it makes owning physical games feel more flexible and inclusive, but it also reminds me of how we used to play back in the day, when you’d share games and play on the couch. You'll need a second Nexus, though, so maybe part of Evercade is happy playing by 2026’s rules.

Two omissions stand out. The big one is HDMI. The original Evercade EXP had a Mini HDMI port; the Nexus is strictly a handheld. You can't plug it into a TV or use it as a portable home console like some rivals.

I get why. Evercade already has the VS and VS-R for TV play, and there's a business case for keeping the products apart. It's still a shame if you want one device to do everything.

The other gap is a proper sleep mode. There's no suspend and resume, so you power down and restart every time you put it away. That feels odd on a modern handheld, especially if you're used to picking up a Switch and jumping straight back in. Boot times aren't speedy either, at around 40 seconds (I know, we're in such a low attention span era, how did I survive ZX Spectrum tape-loading?), so I'd love to see a fix in a future update.

Neither is a deal-breaker. Both remind me that the Nexus is a focused console, and Evercade has clearly balanced features and specs to make it affordable.

Yes, if you want retro gaming without the faff. The Evercade Nexus is the best version of what Evercade has been building towards: comfortable, well-made, with a big screen and a huge library of classics from day one that's dead easy to play.

I like that Evercade hasn't pushed the hardware too far. Banjo-Kazooie shows what the Nexus can do in 3D, and the 8-bit and 16-bit games that built the brand are still right at home.

At $199.99 / £169.99 for the base console ($229.99 / £189.99 for the 64 Edition and $249.99 / £199.99 for the Doom Edition), it isn't competing with cheap emulation handhelds, and it's not directly competitive with Switch 2. The lack of OLED, HDMI and sleep mode will put some people off, but those are clearly choices made to keep Nexus affordable and dodge a headlong fight with Nintendo.

Evercade is selling something different: a curated, licensed, and collectable way to play some of the best games from past arcade and console eras. If you already own cartridges because you have an EXP-R or VS-R, the bigger screen and twin sticks make this a tempting upgrade. If you're new, it's still an easy recommendation.

Ian Dean is Editor, Digital Arts & 3D at Creative Bloq, and the former editor of many leading magazines. These titles included ImagineFX, 3D World and video game titles Play and Official PlayStation Magazine. Ian launched Xbox magazine X360 and edited PlayStation World. For Creative Bloq, Ian combines his experiences to bring the latest news on digital art, VFX and video games and tech, and in his spare time he doodles in Procreate, ArtRage, and Rebelle while finding time to play Xbox and PS5.

Nothing personal says England coach Thomas Tuchel as he overlooks Cole Palmer

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The Chelesa playmaker has been left out of the group to face Croatia and Spain in the Nations League this month, having pulled out of the last squad through injury.

But there are first call-ups for midfielders Josh King and Jobe Bellingham, the brother of England talisman Jude, whilst the Leeds United pair of James Trafford and Dominic Calvert-Lewin have been retained.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Phil Foden has already learnt the high bar Tuchel has for his squad, and Palmer appears to be the latest star name in his bad books.

But the German insists it is not the case.

“I guess everyone is desperate to play for England, and Cole is no exception,” said Tuchel despite his pointed comments during the last camp about how many internationals Palmer had been unavailable for.

“I said last time, he misses too many opportunities to impress. It was a long camp and the boys who were in camp built something extraordinary.

“If you are in camp, this is your chance to impress.

“So it’s not personal, and of course we will never assume that Cole is not in the mindset to come and desperate to play for his country. But the competition is on, and the players who were in camp did an amazing job and kept their place.

“The door is always open, but nothing has changed.”

Palmer was one of five withdrawals ahead of the last camp, along with Declan Rice, Kobbie Mainoo, Tino Livramento and Marcus Rashford. Of them, only Arsenal midfielder Rice is in the current squad.

Palmer has only won five caps since scoring in the 2024 European Championship final.

The younger Bellingham has never played in the Premier League after leaving Sunderland for Borussia Dortmund two summers ago but has established himself as an important player in the Bundesliga.

He and King have benefited from the absence through injury of Alex Scott, and the fact Mainoo only returned to Manchester United's starting XI on Tuesday.

Dean Henderson returns after injury, along with former Leeds loanees Djed Spence and Ben White.

White made himself unavailable following the 2022 World Cup after a reported disagreement with Gareth Southgate's No 2 Steve Holland.

He made himself available once Tuchel took over, and made his return as a 69th-minute substitute against Uruguay in March.

He was booed onto the field that night, and even scoring England's goal in a 1-1 draw did not win over the Wembley crowd.

"We were not happy about this," said Tuchel. "We could understand the disappointment of the fans with what happened before but I think this is expressed now and this should be a closed book.

“I think everyone could see the investment of Ben, the quality of Ben and how he presented himself in the March camp before the World Cup.

“He did excellently, scored for us and then I think he committed a foul and we conceded.

“It was very eventful, not so easy to come back. When I speak to him, I always feel the hunger to come back, the excitement.

“He is the standout player for Arsenal in a position (right-back) where we struggle with injuries somehow and we have a lot of changes.

“Trevoh Chalobah is not in camp so that makes maybe Jarell Quansah half a full-back and half a centre-back in camp and gives Ben the opportunity to impose himself again.

“He deserves to open a new chapter, he deserves positive feedback from the crowd.

“We want everyone to be supported. This is what helps the team.”

England play at home to Croatia on Thursday, and are in Madrid the following Sunday for their final group match of the competition.

What Is Content Provenance? C2PA, Watermarking, and Detecting AI Media

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Content provenance records verifiable information about an asset’s origin and editing history so people and systems can inspect how it was created or changed. This guide explains the mechanism, trade-offs, evaluation, and controls that matter in practice.

Content provenance records verifiable information about an asset's origin and editing history so people and systems can inspect how it was created or changed.

Content provenance deserves a precise explanation because its name identifies a particular information flow, training choice, runtime mechanism, or governance boundary. Treating it as a synonym for “advanced AI” makes claims impossible to test. This guide follows the concept from its input and assumptions through its observable result, then tests the shortcut most likely to be confused with it.

Content provenance records verifiable information about an asset's origin and editing history so people and systems can inspect how it was created or changed. The definition contains three practical commitments: there is an identifiable input, a transformation or decision that is characteristic of Content provenance, and an outcome that can be evaluated against a stated objective. If one of those elements is missing, the label may describe an aspiration rather than an implemented mechanism.

Trustworthy AI requires evidence across the lifecycle. A control is meaningful only when its owner, scope, trigger, expected behavior, and verification method are explicit. For Content provenance, this system view matters because performance can be determined by the surrounding data, interfaces, hardware, permissions, and people even when the underlying model is unchanged. A useful explanation therefore separates the model’s learned behavior from the product that decides when, where, and with what authority that behavior is used.

The nearest misleading shortcut is AI detection that guesses from pixels whether media is synthetic. It may share a visible feature with Content provenance, yet it changes the causal story: different evidence would establish success, different resources would dominate cost, and different controls would prevent harm. The boundary is therefore operational rather than terminological.

The diagram is a compact causal map for Content provenance, not a claim that every implementation uses five software components. Some systems combine stages and others repeat them in a loop. The map remains useful because it forces each change in information or authority to have an owner, an input, an output, and a test.

At this stage of Content provenance, the system must capture assertions at creation or edit time. The useful question is not merely whether that operation occurs, but which information it consumes, which state it changes, and what evidence proves that the change was valid. A reviewer should be able to distinguish the operation from AI detection that guesses from pixels whether media is synthetic and reproduce its result under the same stated conditions.

The handoff into this Content provenance stage begins with the stated objective and should end with a result that can support bind them cryptographically to the asset. Record uncertainty, rejected alternatives, resource use, and any human or software control applied at the boundary. That trace is where teams can detect whether valid provenance describes history but does not prove that the depicted event is true before the same weakness reaches a consequential output.

Record uncertainty, rejected alternatives, resource use, and any human or software control applied at the boundary. At this stage of Content provenance, the system must verify and display the available trust signals. The useful question is not merely whether that operation occurs, but which information it consumes, which state it changes, and what evidence proves that the change was valid. A reviewer should be able to distinguish the operation from AI detection that guesses from pixels whether media is synthetic and reproduce its result under the same stated conditions.

The handoff into this Content provenance stage begins with preserve or update the manifest through transformations and should end with a result that can support monitoring or a final decision. Record uncertainty, rejected alternatives, resource use, and any human or software control applied at the boundary. That trace is where teams can detect whether valid provenance describes history but does not prove that the depicted event is true before the same weakness reaches a consequential output.

Read the Content provenance map forward to understand production and backward to diagnose failure. Forward analysis asks how one stage supplies the next. Backward analysis starts from an incorrect, slow, expensive, or unsafe result and traces which earlier assumption allowed it. The reverse path is often where a team discovers that the decisive error occurred before the model produced anything.

A newsroom photo can carry signed capture and edit assertions while later crops are added to the same provenance chain.

This example is informative because Content provenance can be tied to observable inputs, intermediate states, and an outcome rather than judged through a polished demonstration. A rigorous test would build ordinary, difficult, and deliberately misleading cases around the scenario, preserve a baseline without the technique, and record both average performance and the severity of individual failures.

Change one assumption in the Content provenance example and repeat the analysis. Remove a required input, introduce a conflicting signal, limit compute, alter the user population, or force the system to abstain. A mechanism that only succeeds under one carefully arranged demonstration has not established that it generalizes to the operating environment.

Content provenance is often reduced to AI detection that guesses from pixels whether media is synthetic. That reduction removes the very boundary that defines the concept. It can lead buyers to compare unlike products, researchers to overstate what an experiment demonstrates, and operators to monitor the wrong signal after deployment.

The comparison should also identify the unit of analysis. A paper about Content provenance may isolate a model or algorithm, while a deployed service adds retrieval, routing, caching, policy, identity, user interfaces, and monitoring. Two products can use the same headline term while implementing different parts of that stack. Ask which component performs the defining transformation and which other components are necessary for the reported outcome.

Content provenance matters now because AI systems are being given larger contexts, more modalities, more runtime compute, broader tool access, and deeper connections to organizational decisions. Under those conditions, what once looked like a research detail can determine latency, security, accessibility, environmental cost, product quality, or legal accountability.

The relevant measure is not whether Content provenance can produce one impressive result. It is whether the technique improves an outcome that matters across representative conditions and does so more effectively than a simpler baseline. Report distributions, failure categories, tail latency, resource use, and affected subgroups rather than compressing every result into one average.

Monitor both technical metrics and impacts on people. Document uncertainty, preserve lineage, make escalation possible, and design recovery before the system is exposed to changing real-world conditions. Applied specifically to Content provenance, that discipline makes the evidence portable: another team can judge whether the claimed gain is likely to survive a different model, language, hardware platform, dataset, user population, or risk tolerance.

The strongest reason to use Content provenance is that it can address its intended bottleneck directly. Depending on the implementation, the benefit may appear as better grounding, a more faithful representation, improved generalization, lower latency, reduced memory movement, clearer accountability, or a safer boundary between a model proposal and a real action.

Benefits should be expressed as decisions and measurements. “More intelligent” is not an acceptance criterion for Content provenance. A useful target might specify error rate on hard cases, recovery after conflicting evidence, cost at a percentile of traffic, human-review time, calibration, or the percentage of actions kept within a defined authority limit.

The central limitation is that valid provenance describes history but does not prove that the depicted event is true. This failure is not an afterthought to list once development is complete. It should shape data collection, architecture, permissions, evaluation, release gates, and monitoring for Content provenance from the beginning.

A control for Content provenance is useful only if it acts before an expensive or irreversible consequence. Identify the earliest observable precursor to the failure, set a threshold or rule, assign an accountable owner, and test recovery. Depending on the use case, recovery may mean abstaining, falling back to a simpler system, requesting more evidence, escalating to a person, rolling back a model, or stopping an action entirely.

Begin evaluation of Content provenance by writing the decision the evidence must support. Define the operating population, consequence of a wrong result, information actually available at decision time, and the simplest credible alternative. This prevents a benchmark from becoming the goal simply because it is easy to run.

Use an untouched test set for controlled comparisons, then validate Content provenance in a staged operating environment. Offline evaluation makes variants comparable; shadow mode, canaries, rate limits, or approval gates reveal how real traffic, feedback loops, and people change behavior. The deployment stage should have an explicit stop condition rather than assuming every improvement deserves full rollout.

Version the inputs needed to reproduce Content provenance: source data, preprocessing, tokenizer or encoder, model weights, configuration, prompt or policy, retrieval index, evaluation set, hardware assumptions, and serving code as applicable. Without lineage, a team cannot tell whether a changed result came from the technique, the environment, or an unnoticed pipeline edit.

Finally, ask what finding would falsify the claim that Content provenance helps. If no result could reverse the adoption decision, the evaluation is marketing. Precommitted acceptance thresholds and a preserved confirmation set turn the exercise into evidence.

Authoritative starting points for the part of the AI stack surrounding Content provenance include NIST AI Risk Management Framework, C2PA specifications, NIST Privacy Framework. Read them alongside the documentation for the exact model, dataset, hardware, and jurisdiction involved. A general source can define the mechanism, but only deployment-specific evidence can establish that a particular implementation is suitable.

Content provenance is a defined mechanism inside a larger sociotechnical system. Its value comes from improving a specific outcome under explicit conditions, not from the label itself. The five-stage map makes its information flow visible, the comparison identifies what it is not, and the control path shows where a responsible operator can intervene.

The practical rule for Content provenance is to define the objective, compare against a credible baseline, test the failure that matters most, and retain the evidence needed to monitor change. With those pieces in place, the concept becomes an engineering and governance choice that can be evaluated. Without them, it remains a promising name attached to an unknown operating risk.

Opinion: They've done it, the new Busquets is coming to Liverpool

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Liverpool have done something amazing. They've got the next Sergio Busquets.

It was a sad day for football. Inter Miami's victory over the Vancouver Whitecaps almost exactly a year ago signalled the end of Busquet's career in the game.

What a career it had been. The Spaniard has won pretty much everything and established himself as one of the best holding midfielders to have ever played the sport.

At the height of his powers, playing in that incredible Barcelona team under Pep Guardiola with Xavi and Iniesta, Busquets was unplayable.

He could read the game like no other, and he also had this ability to stay calm under pressure. He was able to command the middle of the park. Mark smart passes and instigate attacks from deep.

Liverpool's need for a midfield metronomeThe Reds could very much do with a player of Busquet's ilk. For a long while now, Liverpool have been desperate to have a midfield metronome. Not since Fabinho have the Reds had a player who is capable of sitting deep in midfield and controlling the middle of the park. They have tried to bring in players of that kind but to no avail. First it was Aurelien Tchouameni. He went to Real Madrid. Moises Caicedo and Romeo Lavia were also pursued. But they left to join Chelsea in what was a bitter blow. Martin Zubimendi, of course, ended up at Arsenal. This summer, Liverpool tried to sign Lamine Camara, but he stayed at Monaco. Perhaps the Reds will explore a move for him in January.

The Reds could very much do with a player of Busquet's ilk. For a long while now, Liverpool have been desperate to have a midfield metronome.

Not since Fabinho have the Reds had a player who is capable of sitting deep in midfield and controlling the middle of the park.

They have tried to bring in players of that kind but to no avail.

First it was Aurelien Tchouameni. He went to Real Madrid. Moises Caicedo and Romeo Lavia were also pursued. But they left to join Chelsea in what was a bitter blow.

Martin Zubimendi, of course, ended up at Arsenal. This summer, Liverpool tried to sign Lamine Camara, but he stayed at Monaco. Perhaps the Reds will explore a move for him in January.

Or perhaps they won't. Liverpool's midfield has become a lot stronger since the summer and that is thanks to the emergence of Trey Nyoni.

The teenage midfielder who has been on the club's books for a few years now has really started to establish himself as an elite ball-playing metronome.

Liverpool look a lot more in control when Nyoni is on the pitch pulling the strings and is able to find the right passes in order to break lines.

There's a lot of Busquets qualities to him, and he's probably the closest thing to the former Barcelona midfielder playing football right now.

He proved that against Arsenal. And he is also proving it in the Champions League. Opta recently revealed that Nyoni has completed the most passes under high-intensity pressure (92%) out of any midfielder in the Champions League to have attempted more than 100 passes this season.

That is a seriously impressive feat, and once again Busquets esque.

Here’s how the midterms could reset Washington’s housing agenda

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Housing affordability is expected to be a top issue for the next Congress. Lawmakers may pursue a ROAD 2.0 package and funding for ROAD programs, while clashing with the Trump administration on tariffs, immigration, and affordable housing spending.  AI Summary

The rising cost of living was a catalyst that drove voters to the polls this week, so housing, which makes up a substantial share of that burden, could gain traction as a top priority once again for the incoming Congress.

Lawmakers in both parties want to address the high cost of housing. The question always is how much they can accomplish. Democrats, who will control the U.S. Senate and the U.S. House of Representatives, may seek common ground with the Trump administration on housing policy, including streamlining regulations and funding the new programs created under the 21st Century ROAD to Housing Act.

However, they could also clash with the administration over hot-button policy debates such as tariffs on building materials, immigration enforcement on construction sites and funding for affordable housing programs, among other issues.

Despite potential areas of agreement and disagreement, Andy Winkler, managing director of housing and infrastructure policy at the Bipartisan Policy Center, said voters’ focus on affordability should keep housing high on the federal policy agenda.

“Housing costs, just like other rising costs of living, were clearly top of mind as voters headed to the polls. So, it appears to me that housing would continue to be a priority in the next Congress because of that,” Winkler said in an interview with HousingWire TBD.

Many housing advocates have pointed to the possibility of a “ROAD 2.0” bill as a follow-up to the 21st Century ROAD to Housing Act, which became law on July 11 after securing overwhelming bipartisan support, with 85 votes in the Senate and 358 votes in the House of Representatives.

Winkler sees significant potential for another bipartisan housing package, despite potential divisions between Congress and the Trump administration. While the original legislation drew on about 60 different bills, many other bipartisan proposals failed to make the final package and could be revisited in the next Congress.

In October, the bipartisan Build America Caucus released a document previewing policy priorities that could shape a second housing bill. The bipartisan agenda included housing proposals aimed at expanding where homes can be built, lowering construction costs and improving access to financing.

The document’s focus points include establishing federally incentivized “Right to Build Zones” with by-right approvals, encouraging housing near transit, opening underused federal properties to residential development, modernizing fragmented building codes, and establishing a federal revolving loan fund for housing projects, among other proposals.

Congress will also welcome an unusually large freshman class, including many Democrats elected in Republican-leaning states and districts. As of Thursday, Democrats had already flipped seven Senate seats, including five in states Trump won by double-digit margins in 2024. Democrats may also pick up still-undecided Senate seats in Mississippi and Alaska, both of which Trump won handily in 2024.

Elections in the U.S. House of Representatives followed a similar pattern, with many Democrats winning in districts that Trump carried handily two years ago. Representing Republican-leaning constituencies could push many of these new members toward a bipartisan agenda, and housing offers a rare opportunity for common ground.

“I think you have a lot of new members coming in who will want to quickly show that they’re being productive and addressing what they ran on,” Winkler said, striking an optimistic tone. “The passage of the 21st Century Road to Housing Act in this Congress shows us that there is common ground on housing policy, and we now also have a model for how to get legislation enacted into law.”

The new Congress will be sworn in on January 3. Until then, Congress will be in a “lame duck” period. Ken Wingert, Chief Advocacy Officer at the National Association of Home Builders (NAHB), wrote in a post-election analysis that Congress will likely begin debating the bipartisan American Affordability and Jobs Act (BAAJA) during the lame duck session, offering a look at another potential federal housing bill.

BAAJA would streamline federal permitting and environmental reviews, lower land development costs and cut down on project delays that prolong the time it takes to secure federal approvals. NAHB argues the reforms could bring more affordable lots to market, accelerate multifamily development and lower costs for projects using federal housing financing and grants.

For his part, James Talarico, the Senator-elect from Texas, signaled during a Wednesday interview on Fox News that he is willing to work with Trump on several issues, including housing affordability.

“I also want to work with the President on housing. He’s a builder, and we need to cut the red tape, the regulations that sometimes my party has been guilty of, so that we can build millions of new homes in this country,” Talarico said.

Trump has also voiced a conciliatory tone since the election, signaling that he is open to working with a Democratic-controlled Congress on bipartisan legislation. Whether that holds true on housing or other critical areas remains to be seen.

“Democrats are expected to focus on increased funding for affordable housing programs, a greater emphasis on workforce training, and limits on President Trump’s tariff and immigration policies that have impacted new construction. At the same time, the Trump administration is expected to continue to pursue an agenda of deregulation that will help spur new housing supply,” Wingert wrote.

Winkler argued that federal funding and budget negotiations could become a major source of both bipartisan agreement and conflict in the next Congress, even if lawmakers can find common ground on housing policy itself.

The 21st Century ROAD to Housing Act requires separate congressional appropriations to fund many of about a dozen newly enacted programs. Examples include the USDA rural housing preservation and rental assistance initiatives. Because of ROAD’s popularity, Congress could widely support appropriating funding for these programs.

“None of [the programs] have funding attached, so it will be a clear priority heading into next year if they want any of those grant programs to exist and actually work in practice,” Winkler explained.

However, disagreements over how to fund affordable housing programs and broader federal spending priorities could create fissures between Democrats and Republicans. Democrats will likely prioritize more funding for affordable housing and homelessness programs. They may also pursue oversight of the administration’s spending, particularly whether the U.S. Department of Housing and Urban Development (HUD) and other agencies are actually distributing housing grant money that Congress has already appropriated.

Winkler also expects scrutiny of HUD’s staffing reductions, which have reportedly cut the agency’s workforce by roughly 30%. The loss of experienced HUD employees could undermine the department’s ability to implement the ROAD to Housing Act’s numerous provisions. However, Congress has limited ability to force the administration to fund and hire more employees, and any legislation would likely still need presidential support.

Democratic leadership has already signaled that they will attempt to take on Trump’s tariff agenda once they assume power in January. It’s unclear, though, how successful that effort will be and if Trump will leverage executive powers to keep certain tariffs in place.

However, Democrats in Congress have already introduced multiple bills to exempt building materials from tariffs in a bid to lower housing costs, including the Housing Tariff Exclusion Act that was introduced earlier this year. NAHB threw its support behind that legislation.

Democrats will likely push for a similar policy of tariff exemptions over the next two years. And, for what it’s worth, the Build America Caucus, which has 21 Republican members, has called for exempting building materials from tariffs as part of its housing policy agenda. If a similar provision is included in a comprehensive follow-up housing bill, it could create a potential policy clash with the Trump administration.

Other policy issues, such as the impact of immigration enforcement on the construction labor pool or a renewed push to expand an already-passed institutional investor ban on purchasing single-family homes, could also create friction between Democrats and Republicans in Congress, and the Trump administration.

Still, bipartisan support is possible in certain areas, like streamlining regulations and funding the programs created by ROAD. However, even where bipartisan support exists, Democratic leadership will, in most cases, need the administration’s backing to turn its priorities into law.

“The president has the veto, so legislation moving forward does need buy-in from everybody,” Winkler said.

Singapore Airlines Unveils New Business Class Suites With Doors, Companion Beds, And More Room To Sleep

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Singapore Airlines has unveiled its next-generation long-haul business class seat, adding sliding doors, redesigned sleeping space, and center seats that can form companion beds. The most interesting change may be the footwell: the airline has redesigned how the table stows to give passengers more room to move their feet while sleeping.

I already wrote about the new first class suites here.

The new business class product was designed by Designworks, a BMW Group company, and manufactured by the German firm RECARO Aircraft Seating. It will be installed on Boeing 777-9 aircraft and retrofitted Airbus A350-900 long-haul and A350-900ULR aircraft. Every seat will have direct aisle access in a 1-2-1 configuration.

The product will not be identical across aircraft. The Boeing version will have a wider seat, while the Airbus version will feature a larger entertainment screen.

Every seat gets a sliding privacy door, with electronically adjustable dividers between the center seats.

The fully flat bed measures 79 inches long and up to 24 inches wide. Selected center seats can be configured as companion beds when the divider is lowered, which appears to be every other row.

The table stows at an angle designed to create a larger footwell, allowing passengers more room to move and reposition their feet–it is the feature I most want to test. Singapore Airlines has a strong service reputation, but its existing long-haul business class seats have divided opinion over their sleeping position and foot space. A door adds privacy, but the ability to stretch out comfortably will matter far more on an overnight flight.

The published bed width is “up to” 24 inches, which I read as saying there will not be a uniform 24-inch-wide sleeping surface from shoulder to foot.

The leather-upholstered seat offers adjustable upper and lower lumbar support and a leg rest easily accessible control buttons should make it easier to adjust the seat without hunting through a touchscreen menu.

Singapore Airlines is also adding a padded bedsheet incorporating memory foam, which it says can provide additional cushioning when sleeping or be used for lumbar support while seated.

Integrated myKrisWorld controls will save a passenger’s preferred seat position for future journeys. Multiple storage compartments, wireless charging, and universal A/C power will be included.

On the A350, the seat is 22 inches wide, with a 22-inch 4K OLED screen. On the 777-9, the seat measures 24 inches across, but the screen is a smaller 20-inch 4K display.

Both offer Bluetooth headphone pairing and supplied noise-canceling headphones. The entertainment handset also displays flight information, destination weather, Wi-Fi status, and lavatory availability.

The new cabins are scheduled to enter service progressively from the second half of 2027.

Singapore Airlines has also announced a progressive Starlink rollout on its A350 long-haul and ULR aircraft from early 2027. That is a separate installation program, so new connectivity will likely occur before the aircraft has new seats.

Singapore Airlines’ new business class addresses several features passengers now expect, including privacy doors and Bluetooth audio. The redesigned footwell and additional bedding interest me more than the doors. If the seat delivers a comfortable lounging position and an easier night’s sleep, this could be a substantial improvement. Like with first class, I look forward to finding out onboard.

LIVE- Wall Street Power Grab? Anti-Crypto Elizabeth Warren Could Put Day Trading in the Crosshairs

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Elizabeth Warren is preparing to take control of the Senate Banking Committee, putting one of Washington’s most outspoken financial regulators at the center of America’s next market policy battle. Her expected appointment could bring renewed scrutiny to Wall Street, crypto, and the rules governing retail day trading.

With Democrats taking control of both chambers of Congress in the November 2026 midterms, Warren will have a stronger platform to investigate financial firms and challenge federal regulators. Her priorities could extend beyond traditional banking, bringing day trading platforms, market structure, and prediction markets into the congressional red-list.

JUST IN: 🇺🇸 Anti-crypto Senator Elizabeth Warren expected to become Chair of Senate Banking Committee, giving her major power over the financial industry. pic.twitter.com/t2nvMSdo1U

The Massachusetts senator has spent years arguing that financial markets need stronger protections for ordinary Americans. Now, she could have the committee authority to turn those arguments into hearings, investigations, and legislative proposals.

The connection to day trading is not simply about whether Americans can buy and sell stocks freely. It is about the market infrastructure that determines how retail orders are executed, priced, and routed.

Elizabeth Warren has already taken a position on one important part of that debate. On October 1, she joined four Democratic senators in urging SEC Chairman Paul Atkins to preserve key Regulation National Market System protections.

The lawmakers opposed proposed changes to Rules 611 and 610(e), warning that removing protections could allow retail orders to execute at worse prices. They argued that larger market participants could benefit while ordinary investors faced higher trading costs.

For day trading platforms and their customers, these rules matter. Fast-moving traders depend on competitive execution, transparent pricing and reliable access to market liquidity.

Warren’s intervention shows that retail trading infrastructure is already on her regulatory agenda. Her committee leadership could give her more opportunities to question the SEC about how brokers, exchanges and market makers handle individual investors’ orders.

This does not mean Warren has proposed restrictions on day trading itself. There is currently no confirmed proposal from her to limit how frequently retail investors can trade.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Day trading is only one potential part of Warren’s expanding financial oversight agenda. The senator has also raised concerns about prediction markets, particularly the possibility of insider trading involving contracts tied to political and economic events.

The Wall Street Journal reported that Warren intends to examine alleged insider trading in prediction markets alongside private equity, major banks and crypto companies. Meanwhile, Democrats are preparing investigations into financial relationships between the crypto industry, Trump and his family.

Warren is expected to lead the Senate Banking Committee and has pledged to examine alleged deals connecting the industry with the president.

Senator Elizabeth Warren accuses President Donald Trump of using his presidency to benefit wealthy donors and corporate allies at the expense of ordinary Americans. In a video posted on X, Warren points to corporate donations, dropped lawsuits, cryptocurrency ventures and airline… pic.twitter.com/bQ1twXJfvN

Those investigations could put companies with political and financial connections under greater pressure. They could also consume congressional attention that the crypto industry had hoped to devote to market structure legislation.

On day trading businesses, the consequences may be less direct but still important. Congressional scrutiny of retail trading protections, prediction markets, and financial intermediaries could influence how regulators approach trading platforms.

Warren’s critics may argue that additional regulation risks increasing compliance costs and limiting competition. Her supporters, meanwhile, point to investor protections and fair market access as reasons for stronger oversight.

The bigger question is how far Elizabeth Warren intends to take that agenda once she assumes the committee chairmanship. With subpoena powers, control over hearings, and a stronger legislative platform, she will have more influence over the financial industry’s relationship with federal regulators.

Democrats Shouldn’t Squander Their Chance to Launch a Foreign Policy Revolution

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Since its founding in 1922, Foreign Affairs has been the leading forum for serious discussion of American foreign policy and global affairs. The magazine has featured contributions from many leading international affairs experts.

MATTHEW DUSS is Executive Vice President of the Center for International Policy. From 2017 to 2022, he served as Chief Foreign Policy Adviser to Senator Bernie Sanders.

The Imperative of Ambition in a New Congress

Tuesday’s election made clear that endless wars, political corruption, and the high cost of living are three of the American public’s top concerns. They are deeply interrelated—and the new Congress must address all three. That will require a true foreign policy revolution that grapples not just with the outrages and failures of the Trump administration’s foreign policy but also with the ways in which past Democratic or Democrat-supported foreign policies have contributed to today’s crisis. There will be a temptation among some Democratic leaders, in Congress and beyond, to limit the scope or pace of change. But to find a way out of today’s crisis and move beyond the failures that have afflicted American foreign policy for the past several decades, Democrats will need to have the courage to face a hard truth: that President Donald Trump’s abuses of power and unilateral warmaking are not so much a break from Washington’s militarist mindset as they are a particularly aggressive, predatory expression of it.

The incoming Democratic majority will face a national security environment in which the most severe challenges to U.S. safety and prosperity come from within. That’s why Congress should shut down the Iran war immediately but also recognize that it can make the United States more secure only if it pairs a foreign policy shift with a frontal assault on spiraling costs and political corruption. Political polarization; government corruption and dysfunction; increasing dominance of political and economic life by a small handful of mercurial oligarchs; and a president who recklessly and unilaterally launches unauthorized military interventions while relentlessly seeking opportunities to enrich himself, his family, and his cronies—all pose more immediate danger to the safety and prosperity of the American people than does any foreign enemy.

When Democrats take control of Congress in January, they will have considerable power to challenge the corruption and malfeasance of the Trump administration. Control of committees and of the congressional calendar will provide the opportunity for sorely needed oversight. But although the Trump administration deserves aggressive investigation, Democrats must aim higher—and use the opportunity to articulate a genuinely compelling alternative for American foreign policy.

The first and most urgent task will be ending the Iran war, a reckless and unpopular conflict that has become even less popular as Americans have felt its effects on their wallets. Congress must respond to widespread bipartisan anger among voters at Trump’s war and abuse of war powers by using all the tools it has, especially its power to cut funding. It has been encouraging to see Democrats continuing to oppose any supplemental budget request by the administration for the war. It is an area where Democrats have gotten tripped up in the past when they’ve been afraid of accusations that they don’t “support the troops” if they oppose funding for foolish, counterproductive wars. With Democrats having held firm against any additional funding for the war, those days appear to be gone. That is good.

But Democrats need to go further. They should be prepared to oppose all funding for the military, including blocking votes on the National Defense Authorization Act, the annual bill setting out the broad scope and budget for the Defense Department, in order to put an end to hostilities in Iran. In fact, not only should they be willing to withhold funding from the Pentagon, but they should also be willing to withhold funding for the entire government, and threaten another government shutdown if the bill does not prohibit any funding for Trump’s war. December 11 is the deadline for a continuing resolution to keep the government funded and operating. If ever there was a reason for Democrats to threaten to withhold their votes, stopping a blatantly illegal and globally destructive war that American voters have now resoundingly rebuked is it.

Democrats must also be willing to take a hard look at the continuity between Trump’s use of military violence and past uses of force by administrations of both parties, and the bipartisan support for that violence. The groundwork for the Iran war was laid by the Biden administration’s unstinting support for Israel’s genocide in Gaza (as even some former administration officials have acknowledged), but also by years of U.S. refusal to impose any consequences on the Israeli government despite clear evidence of ongoing violations of human rights and international law. Trump’s strikes on alleged “narcoterrorist” drug boats in the Caribbean represent the logical evolution of long-standing treatment of the United States’ drug problem as a “war,” and of casually applying the label of “terrorist” to justify any and all measures, including execution without due process. Seeking accountability for these abuses will require acknowledging the past complicity of both Republicans and Democrats.

Democrats must also take on corruption, a key node connecting the country’s domestic and foreign policy crises. In doing so, they will be walking through an open door. Polls consistently show that political corruption is high on Americans’ list of concerns. According to a recent Harris poll conducted for The Guardian, “Nearly nine out of 10 Americans said that government corruption, as defined as elected officials putting their own or their donors’ financial interests ahead of the public’s, is a problem.” A mid-October CNBC All-America Economic Survey found that voters are more concerned about government corruption than any other issue, including high grocery and gas prices. In September, Gallup reported that “Americans’ perceptions of government corruption in the U.S. are at their highest level in 20 years, with 89% of U.S. adults saying it is widespread.”

To say that the Trump administration provides a target-rich environment for a strong anticorruption push is to commit felony understatement. Exposing foreign gifts to Trump and conflicts of interest by family members, contracts awarded to Trump’s oligarch donors and new tech buddies, and the selling of pardons, as well as restoring enforcement of the Corporate Transparency Act, the Foreign Corrupt Practices Act, and related task forces, should all be part of the plan. Democrats should drill down into Trump’s wildly corrupt “Board of Peace” for Gaza, which U.S. Representative Jamie Raskin, the Maryland Democrat set to chair the House Judiciary Committee, rightly referred to as a “global slush fund.” The obvious conflicts of interests of Trump’s diplomatic envoys Steve Witkoff and Jared Kushner also demand investigation.

The good news is that effective anticorruption does not necessarily require new legislation; it merely requires that existing legislation be enforced. An August 2025 essay in Foreign Affairs, co-authored with my colleagues Casey Michel and Trevor Sutton, laid out a more expansive case for Democrats putting anticorruption at the center of their foreign policy platform. (A recent essay by Casey Michel lays out a more detailed agenda for the new Congress.)

Ultimately, the most important step toward tackling corruption will be reforming the campaign finance system. The United States is an outlier among developed democracies in that its system of campaign finance allows for what is essentially legalized bribery. Legislators speak openly about campaign contributors expecting “returns on investment” for their political donations. This is an outrage. In recent years, foreign actors have increasingly begun bankrolling American legislators, taking advantage of massive loopholes in campaign finance laws and the Federal Election Commission’s lack of enforcement.

Confronting this will be no small task. At a minimum, Congress must move to legislate a response to the Supreme Court’s 2010 Citizens United decision, which effectively greenlighted bottomless campaign funding from American corporations. It must also reform the Federal Election Commission, providing the body with much-needed resources to investigate campaign finance crimes and restoring the practice of allowing congressional appointments to the commission, which has become little more than an appendage of the executive branch.

All of the above steps must be connected to the top concern among American voters at this moment—the high cost of living. Democrats have disagreements in several policy areas, particularly in foreign policy. Their coalition is diverse. But there is consensus around addressing Americans’ material needs urgently and aggressively. Such an approach would confront Trump’s reckless use of tariffs (while recognizing that the neoliberal taboo on tariffs is a thing of the past), rising gas prices because of the Iran war, the need to restore the global minimum tax, the threat of job losses to AI, and the increased power of tech oligarchs over Americans’ economic and political lives, including on foreign policy, as Elon Musk’s restriction of Ukraine’s Starlink connection demonstrated. The approach should involve a mix of tactics, including hearings, oversight investigations, and legislative proposals.

Many of these efforts may not produce new laws in the next two years. But they could become the backbone of a winning agenda for Congress and for a Democratic administration in 2029. Most important, they would represent a step toward the forging of a new American political consensus on which a new and durable foreign policy can be built.
