Democrats Shouldn’t Squander Their Chance to Launch a Foreign Policy Revolution

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Since its founding in 1922, Foreign Affairs has been the leading forum for serious discussion of American foreign policy and global affairs. The magazine has featured contributions from many leading international affairs experts.

MATTHEW DUSS is Executive Vice President of the Center for International Policy. From 2017 to 2022, he served as Chief Foreign Policy Adviser to Senator Bernie Sanders.

The Imperative of Ambition in a New Congress

Tuesday’s election made clear that endless wars, political corruption, and the high cost of living are three of the American public’s top concerns. They are deeply interrelated—and the new Congress must address all three. That will require a true foreign policy revolution that grapples not just with the outrages and failures of the Trump administration’s foreign policy but also with the ways in which past Democratic or Democrat-supported foreign policies have contributed to today’s crisis. There will be a temptation among some Democratic leaders, in Congress and beyond, to limit the scope or pace of change. But to find a way out of today’s crisis and move beyond the failures that have afflicted American foreign policy for the past several decades, Democrats will need to have the courage to face a hard truth: that President Donald Trump’s abuses of power and unilateral warmaking are not so much a break from Washington’s militarist mindset as they are a particularly aggressive, predatory expression of it.

The incoming Democratic majority will face a national security environment in which the most severe challenges to U.S. safety and prosperity come from within. That’s why Congress should shut down the Iran war immediately but also recognize that it can make the United States more secure only if it pairs a foreign policy shift with a frontal assault on spiraling costs and political corruption. Political polarization; government corruption and dysfunction; increasing dominance of political and economic life by a small handful of mercurial oligarchs; and a president who recklessly and unilaterally launches unauthorized military interventions while relentlessly seeking opportunities to enrich himself, his family, and his cronies—all pose more immediate danger to the safety and prosperity of the American people than does any foreign enemy.

When Democrats take control of Congress in January, they will have considerable power to challenge the corruption and malfeasance of the Trump administration. Control of committees and of the congressional calendar will provide the opportunity for sorely needed oversight. But although the Trump administration deserves aggressive investigation, Democrats must aim higher—and use the opportunity to articulate a genuinely compelling alternative for American foreign policy.

The first and most urgent task will be ending the Iran war, a reckless and unpopular conflict that has become even less popular as Americans have felt its effects on their wallets. Congress must respond to widespread bipartisan anger among voters at Trump’s war and abuse of war powers by using all the tools it has, especially its power to cut funding. In fact, not only should they be willing to withhold funding from the Pentagon, but they should also be willing to withhold funding for the entire government, and threaten another government shutdown if the bill does not prohibit any funding for Trump’s war. December 11 is the deadline for a continuing resolution to keep the government funded and operating. Seeking accountability for these abuses will require acknowledging the past complicity of both Republicans and Democrats.

Architectures of Movement: 6 Unbuilt Transport Structures from the ArchDaily Community

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The idea of transportation infrastructure tends to be more hostile than interesting: spaces of transit, of constant encounters with strangers, places that no one seems to claim. These structures organize movement and provide spaces that often operate around the clock. The unbuilt projects below push back against this notion of a no-man's-land, proposing infrastructure as an object of architecture. Located in Iran, Montenegro, the Canary Islands, Sweden, the United Arab Emirates, and South Korea, these project ideas offer a look at the culture and image of their surroundings. Designed for trucks, cars, planes, and pedestrians, they provide not only a structure but also an atmosphere for service, waiting, or encounter. Along the way, they offer fresh images for spaces that often go unnoticed.

The idea of transportation infrastructure tends to be more hostile than interesting: spaces of transit, of constant encounters with strangers, places that no one seems to claim. These structures organize movement and provide spaces that often operate around the clock. The unbuilt projects below push back against this notion of a no-man's-land, proposing infrastructure as an object of architecture. Located in Iran, Montenegro, the Canary Islands, Sweden, the United Arab Emirates, and South Korea, these project ideas offer a look at the culture and image of their surroundings. Designed for trucks, cars, planes, and pedestrians, they provide not only a structure but also an atmosphere for service, waiting, or encounter. Along the way, they offer fresh images for spaces that often go unnoticed.

Transportation infrastructure involves logistical challenges that risk taking priority over aesthetic or environmental criteria. In contrast, this selection of designs shared by ArchDaily users shows experience and relationship to the landscape treated as design criteria. The projects are grounded in geography, drawing on graphic, material, and climatic elements. A port truck terminal shaped like a rock formation, a service station inspired by an eagle's wings, an island airport along the line of the horizon, an intermodal station with an earthen facade, an aviation terminal as fluid as water, and a bus stop that responds to the seasons all recall the classic idea of public architecture drawing inspiration from nature.

Opinion: Wow, they are here, the new Roberto Firmino and Mo Salah have arrived

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A new duo are on the scene, replicating Mo Salah and Roberto Firmino.

Things were different when you tuned into every Liverpool match and these two would be lining up for the Reds.

Firmino smiling pearly whites glaring up at the screen. His twinkle touches and mesmerising movements. Combine that with his silky skills and tireless running.

The Brazilian made things look simple. But he was the glue in the spine of Jurgen Klopp's team. Without Firmino, there is simply no Liverpool success.

Mo Salah is the same. He was different. Explosive and clinical. Unplayable in his prime. Salah was very much the star of the team. The Egyptian King, who we all adored and loved.

He broke records and became one of the most important faces of the club's history in the modern era.

Liverpool's attack miss Firmino and SalahThe most free-flowing attacking football the Reds played was when both of them were still at the club. Firmino departed in 2023, and then Salah has followed this summer. Liverpool have some exciting attackers. Expensive attackers, too. They have all proven themselves at the highest level. But the reality is that what we have seen (so far) is nowhere near at the same level as Salah and Firmino. Sure, Alexander Isak is scoring a lot of goals. Bradley Barcola has moments of flair, and Rio Ngumoha is an extremely tricky winger. Meanwhile, Victor Munoz is hard to contain when he's playing his best football. However, the chemistry between them just isn't there yet. Over time, it might be. Andoni Iraola is undoubtedly working on that in the training ground.

The most free-flowing attacking football the Reds played was when both of them were still at the club. Firmino departed in 2023, and then Salah has followed this summer.

Liverpool have some exciting attackers. Expensive attackers, too. They have all proven themselves at the highest level.

But the reality is that what we have seen (so far) is nowhere near at the same level as Salah and Firmino. Sure, Alexander Isak is scoring a lot of goals.

Bradley Barcola has moments of flair, and Rio Ngumoha is an extremely tricky winger. Meanwhile, Victor Munoz is hard to contain when he's playing his best football.

However, the chemistry between them just isn't there yet. Over time, it might be. Andoni Iraola is undoubtedly working on that in the training ground.

The new Firmino and Salah have arrivedNaturally, Liverpool's recruitment team will also be continuously looking at the market. If things don't work out, you can always replace players and bring new ones in. A team is never complete. Not in modern football. You constantly have to evolve and give new arsenal to your managers. Two players who Liverpool might be looking at are Nico Paz and Anastasios Douvikas. The former has 14 goal contributions in 13 matches this season. Meanwhile, the latter has 12 in 14 matches. Both are playing for Como at the moment, and recently, they achieved a feat that only Salah and Firmino have done together in the past. According to WhoScored, Douvikas and Paz are the first teammates to both achieve a 10.0 match rating in the same UEFA Champions League fixture since April 2018 when Salah and Firmino achieved that feat against AS Roma. Perhaps, it's time to bring both of them to Anfield.

Why Businesses Should Validate AI Use Cases Before Investing in Development

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Artificial intelligence is becoming part of everyday business planning. Companies are exploring ways to automate repetitive tasks, improve customer service, analyze information, and reduce operational costs.

But there is an important difference between finding an interesting AI idea and identifying a problem that AI can actually solve.

Many businesses start discussing development before they fully understand what they want to improve. They select a technology, estimate a budget, and begin building a solution without checking whether the idea makes business sense.

This can lead to expensive projects that work technically but deliver little practical value.

Before spending money on AI development, businesses need to validate whether the proposed use case solves a real problem, has the right data, and can produce measurable results.

Taking time to answer these questions can prevent unnecessary spending and help companies focus on opportunities worth developing.

Every useful AI project should begin with a problem that employees or customers already experience.

A company might say it wants an AI chatbot because competitors are using one. But that does not explain what the chatbot needs to accomplish.

Are customers waiting too long for answers? Are support employees repeatedly handling the same questions? Are important requests being missed outside business hours?

Each problem requires a different approach.

For example, if customers cannot find basic order information, improving the existing customer portal may solve the issue without developing an AI assistant.

However, if support employees spend several hours answering varied questions across multiple systems, an AI solution could be worth exploring.

The first step is to understand the current process, identify where difficulties occur, and determine why they happen.

Without this understanding, businesses risk investing in technology that does not address the original problem.

Not every business activity needs artificial intelligence.

Some tasks follow simple rules and can be handled using traditional software automation. Others involve understanding documents, recognizing patterns, or working with large amounts of information.

The difference matters when deciding where to invest.

Consider a finance department that receives hundreds of invoices every week.

If employees simply copy information between two compatible systems, a standard integration may be enough.

But if invoices arrive in different formats and employees must read, classify, and extract information before entering it into accounting software, AI-powered document processing may offer greater value.

The business should examine how often the task occurs, how much time it requires, and what difficulties employees face.

Good opportunities are often found in processes involving repeated manual reviews, large document volumes, or information that takes too long to organize.

The objective is not to introduce AI everywhere. It is to find situations where its capabilities provide a meaningful advantage.

A problem can be frustrating without being expensive or important enough to justify a new AI solution.

For example, employees might spend ten minutes each week preparing a small report. Automating that task could save time, but the development and maintenance costs may exceed the benefit.

Compare this with a logistics company where employees spend several hours every day checking shipment documents.

Delays in this process could affect customer communication, billing, and delivery schedules.

Improving that workflow may provide a stronger business case.

Companies should consider how frequently the problem occurs, how many people it affects, and what happens when the process is delayed or completed incorrectly.

They should also examine whether the problem affects revenue, operating costs, customer satisfaction, or compliance.

A useful AI use case should address a problem that matters to business performance, not simply an inconvenience that technology could remove.

An AI solution cannot perform reliably without access to suitable information.

Businesses sometimes assume that because they have years of customer records, invoices, or operational reports, their data is ready for AI development.

Information may be stored across multiple applications, contain missing fields, or follow inconsistent formats.

For example, a company planning an AI sales forecasting system may discover that customer records are incomplete and sales reports are updated differently across departments.

These issues can affect the quality of forecasts, even when the underlying technology works correctly.

Before approving development, teams need to confirm where the required data exists, whether it is accurate, and how it can be accessed.

They must also understand whether privacy or security requirements restrict its use.

Sometimes, improving data quality is the most valuable first step. Starting development before completing that work may lead to delays and additional costs.

A promising use case may still be difficult to implement if it does not fit into the company’s existing operations.

Businesses depend on ERP platforms, CRM tools, accounting systems, and other applications that employees use every day.

Introducing AI often means connecting these systems and changing how information moves between teams.

Imagine a company developing an AI tool to process purchase orders.

The tool may successfully extract order information, but employees still need a way to review exceptions, approve transactions, and update the ERP system.

If these steps are not planned, the solution may create another disconnected process.

Before committing to development, businesses should identify which systems AI needs to access, what information it must exchange, and where employee approval is required.

A use case becomes more practical when the complete workflow is understood, not just the individual AI task.

AI development requires more than an initial software budget.

Companies may need to pay for integration, data preparation, testing, employee training, security reviews, and ongoing maintenance.

These expenses should be compared with realistic business benefits.

For example, suppose a team spends 200 hours each month reviewing documents. If an AI solution could reduce the manual workload by 80 hours, the company can estimate the value of that time.

However, saved hours do not automatically become financial savings. The business must consider whether employees can use the time for other valuable work or whether costs will actually decrease.

It is also important to include the time employees will spend reviewing incorrect outputs and handling exceptions.

Businesses developing a wider adoption plan can use an enterprise AI roadmap focused on use cases and ROI to understand how business value, implementation readiness, and risk can guide investment decisions.

A realistic business case helps decision-makers compare opportunities instead of approving projects based on assumptions.

One of the most effective ways to validate an AI use case is to test it before committing to full development.

A pilot allows businesses to examine whether the proposed solution can handle real tasks using a limited amount of data and a controlled workflow.

For example, a company planning an AI document processing system might begin with one invoice type from a small group of suppliers.

The team can measure extraction accuracy, processing time, and the number of documents requiring manual correction.

This provides practical evidence about what works and what needs improvement.

A pilot should also test difficult situations, such as incomplete documents, unusual formats, and missing information.

The aim is not to create a perfect demonstration. It is to understand whether the solution can perform reliably under normal business conditions.

If the pilot reveals serious problems, the company can adjust its approach before spending more money.

Some AI applications support employees by preparing information or suggesting actions. Others may influence decisions involving customers, finances, or sensitive records.

These use cases require different levels of control.

For example, using AI to summarize internal meeting notes carries different risks from using it to approve financial transactions.

Before development begins, businesses should examine what could happen if the system produces an incorrect result.

Could it create financial losses? Could customers receive misleading information? Could confidential data become exposed?

Teams should decide where human review is necessary and how mistakes will be corrected.

They should also confirm who will be responsible for monitoring the system after deployment.

Identifying these risks early makes it easier to plan appropriate safeguards instead of adding them after problems occur.

Businesses often have several possible AI opportunities across sales, finance, operations, and customer support.

Choosing the first idea discussed in a meeting may mean overlooking a simpler project with greater potential.

A better approach is to compare opportunities using the same criteria.

Each proposed use case can be reviewed against five practical questions:

This comparison helps businesses identify opportunities that offer useful benefits without unnecessary complexity.

A smaller project with reliable data and clear results may be a better starting point than a large initiative involving several departments.

The strongest use case is not always the most ambitious one. It is the one the organization can realistically implement, manage, and improve.

Validating a use case does not always lead to approval.

Sometimes, the right decision is to delay development until the business has better data, stronger processes, or clearer objectives.

In other situations, traditional software may solve the problem at a lower cost.

Rejecting an AI idea does not mean the company is falling behind.

It means the organization is making a decision based on business needs rather than pressure to adopt new technology.

A useful validation process should allow teams to proceed, revise the idea, postpone it, or stop it completely.

This flexibility protects resources and leaves room for stronger opportunities.

AI can help businesses reduce repetitive work, improve access to information, and support better decisions. But those benefits are not guaranteed simply because a company invests in development.

The results depend heavily on choosing the right problem.

Before approving an AI project, businesses should understand the existing workflow, check data readiness, estimate potential value, review implementation challenges, and test important assumptions.

They should also be willing to explore simpler alternatives when AI is not necessary.

The purpose of validating AI use cases is not to slow down innovation. It is to make sure time, money, and effort are invested in solutions that can deliver genuine business value.

Companies that make these decisions early are better prepared to develop practical AI solutions that employees can use and the business can sustain.

Shashank Jaiswal is the Co-Founder and Chief Information Officer at SDLC Corp, with more than 10 years of experience in enterprise technology consulting, solution architecture, and software implementation. His work covers ERP and CRM systems, SaaS development, workflow automation, and custom enterprise software. He focuses on aligning scalable technology architectures with business requirements and helping organizations modernize their enterprise systems.

Singapore Airlines Unveils Posh New First Class Suites With Double Beds For 777-9 And A350ULR

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Singapore Airlines has unveiled its next-generation first class seat, with sliding doors, companion dining, and center seats that combine into a double bed measuring five feet across. The new product will appear on the Airbus A350-900ULR and Boeing 777-9, bringing first class to the airline’s ultra-long-range A350 fleet for the first time.

I also wrote about the carrier’s new business class product here.

Singapore Airlines’ new first class specifications reveal a small cabin with substantial personal space. The A350-900ULR will have four seats, while the Boeing 777-9 will have six. Both use a 1-2-1 arrangement with direct aisle access and a curved cabin layout.

PriestmanGoode designed the product, with Collins Aerospace manufacturing the seats. The interiors feature Poltrona Frau leather and padded walls incorporating Singapore Airlines’ batik motif.

The centerpiece is the pair of center seats. A fully retractable divider allows passengers to share the space, with the seats moving together to create either a two-person sofa or a double bed. That bed measures 80 inches long and 60 inches wide. Singapore Airlines describes it as the largest double bed in commercial aviation.

Solo passengers receive an 80-inch-long, 30-inch-wide bed. The seat itself measures 23 inches across with the armrests raised, expanding to 30 inches when they are lowered.

Every seat also supports face-to-face dining with a companion, a feature I appreciate in first class: traveling together should include the option to sit across from one another for dinner, even when you prefer separate window seats.

The seat includes electronically adjustable side wings, upper and lower lumbar support, adjustable armrests, a leg rest, and an ottoman. Integrated myKrisWorld controls can save preferred seat positions and lighting settings for future flights.

For sleeping, the crew will prepare the bed with cotton linens, a duvet, a choice of pillows, and a new mattress topper from German bedding company Paradies. Singapore Airlines says the topper uses breathable spacer fabric to improve cushioning and temperature regulation.

Dedicated storage includes a personal wardrobe, a leather-lined vanity drawer, and space for cabin baggage beneath the ottoman.

Both aircraft receive a 32-inch 4K entertainment screen, but the A350ULR version is OLED. The 777-9 specification lists a conventional 4K display.

Passengers will be offered Bang & Olufsen wireless headphones or can pair Bluetooth headphones with the system. A separate touch panel displays flight information, connectivity status, and lavatory availability without interrupting the main screen.

The new cabins are scheduled to begin entering service in the second half of 2027. Unlike with ANA, the unveiling does not signal an imminent onboard debut. First class is planned for the A350-900ULR and 777-9. The standard long-haul A350-900 will receive the new business class, but no first class cabin.

Singapore Airlines’ new first class looks lovely, particularly for couples who can use the center seats as a shared living and sleeping space. Companion dining at every seat and substantial bedding are welcome details too.

At least 10 local governments have moved to drop Flock’s surveillance cameras since summer

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

At least 10 local governments and police departments have moved since this summer to curb, restrict, or end their use of Flock Safety technology as concerns about mass surveillance and data privacy continue to grow. The backlash is part of a broader pushback against the rapid adoption of advanced AI technology, including the data center buildout, facial recognition, and other uses of AI.

Flock, founded in 2017, sells surveillance software — like license plate readers, drones, and microphone-based edge-computing devices to pick up gunshots and other sounds — that can help solve crimes, such as finding missing people or stolen cars. More than 100,000 Flock cameras are in use nationwide.

But in the past three months, Monroe, Ohio; Newburgh, New York; Richmond, Virginia; Hopkinton, Massachusetts; New Bedford, Massachusetts; and Lansing, Michigan, have all moved to end their contracts with Flock. Meanwhile, police in East Bridgewater and Grafton, Massachusetts, also announced plans to stop using Flock cameras. Just this week, residents of League City, Texas, voted against their police department’s continued use of Flock’s license plate readers, and the cameras are now set to be removed.

The concerns range from privacy to how data is shared (and with whom) to procurement concerns.

In Asheville, North Carolina, police lost access to Flock after the city voted to terminate its contract in August, and the El Paso County Sheriff’s Office in Colorado announced in September that it would stop using Flock cameras. When asked for comment, the El Paso County Sheriff’s Office redirected TechCrunch to its press release, where Sheriff Joseph Roybal said that discontinuing the use of Flock was the right decision for the county.

“We have the tools, training, and experience to locate and apprehend criminals without relying on Flock cameras,” he said in the press release.

Tatiana Rice, a senior director for U.S. legislation at The Future of Privacy Forum, told TechCrunch that the backlash to Flock isn’t surprising and only further represents the “growing public skepticism toward technology.” Flock is a target because people can see the sensors and cameras as they drive along roads; data centers have also become physical embodiments of the resentment growing against emerging technology. “A camera at the end of your street is tangible in a way that everyday sharing and selling of personal data online is not,” Rice said.

At the state level this summer, for example, Florida barred license plate readers from state highways, while Texas paused state funding for Flock. The issues again range from data privacy concerns to misuse of the technology.

Misuse has been a real problem. In August, for example, an officer in Brentwood, Missouri was accused of using Flock cameras to stalk his ex-wife during their divorce. In September, an officer from East Texas was sentenced to prison for using Flock to stalk his ex-girlfriend and her family.

Another growing concern is how immigration officials have been able to access the technology. One case that captures the issue came in March, when Ithaca, New York, passed a resolution to terminate its contract with the Flock Group.

In the resolution, the city said there were “proven instances of Flock Safety infrastructure being used by federal agents for information purposes even without specific authorizations from the contracting locality,” and that the city had reviewed reports that “either intentionally or through its negligence,” Flock had allowed U.S. Immigration and Customs Enforcement (ICE) and the Bureau of Alcohol, Tobacco, Firearms and Explosives (ATF) to “use query results from its database of state and local ALPR [automatic license plate reader] data to investigate and persecute immigrants throughout the United States.”

“Every day, police departments across the country are using Flock technology to solve serious crimes, locate suspects, find missing people, and make a measurable impact on public safety,” Flock told TechCrunch in a statement. “While we respect every community’s decision, removing a tool that is actively helping solve violent crimes and protect communities moves public safety backward. Every Flock customer owns its data and controls who it is shared with and how it can be used. Flock does not sell data and does not have a contract with ICE.”

This August, Flock announced it would shorten the default period it stores data for new customers, from 30 days to seven. It also said agencies now need a case code before they can search the database.

The conversation around Flock echoes, in many ways, the fight over data centers. Many of these cities adopted Flock just a few years ago, and residents likely had no idea it was being installed, Adam Schwartz of the Electronic Frontier Foundation, a digital privacy nonprofit that has investigated Flock before, told TechCrunch.

That lack of awareness is similar to how data centers are popping up across the country, often before residents understand what is happening. Schwartz said police departments often decide to use Flock on their own, with little oversight from city councils. In South Windsor, Connecticut, according to local reports, Flock cameras went live last year without public warning or a vote. In October, the town voted to remove its Flock cameras.

“Grassroots activists across the country have been demanding that city councils exercise their procurement power to refuse to pay for ALPRs,” Schwartz said, referring to councils’ power to decide what the city buys. He added that activists have been fighting for years and that “many city councils are listening.”

The main argument for technology like Flock is that it helps combat crime and keep neighborhoods safe. And many local governments are not dropping Flock or similar technology; instead, they are adding restrictions on its use or looking for alternatives.

Cleveland, Ohio, for example, voted this summer to extend its contract with Flock, but has started restricting searches related to immigration. It has also limited access to the database to the Cleveland Police alone, cutting off the Northeast Ohio Regional Fusion Center, essentially a federal intelligence agency, which had initially been given access.

“They are used and needed for investigative purposes,” the city said when reached for comment. “The Department of Public Safety is currently reviewing responses to an RFP [request for proposals] that was sent out to other vendors that provide the same technology as Flock.”

But as with data centers, communities want more say in the trade-offs. “These tools can offer real public safety benefits, but communities are clearly interested in ensuring that those benefits are not at the cost of their civil liberties,” Rice said. Surveillance technology will likely remain a political hot topic, especially since Democrats now control Congress.

In September, President Donald Trump said he liked Flock’s technology “because of law enforcement,” even though a Reuters poll that same month found that more Americans dislike the technology than like it.

Also that month, Rep. Thomas Massie, a Republican from Kentucky, introduced a bill called the Flock-Off Act to bar the use of federal money to buy “Flock-style surveillance” and put other safeguards in place. In October, Sen. Bernie Sanders (I-Vt.), Rep. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez (D-N.Y.), and Sen. Jeff Merkley (D-Ore.) introduced the Ban Flock Act to end “the rise of AI-powered surveillance cameras.”

Lamine Yamal takes Mbappé’s Champions League under-20 scoring record as Barça beat Villa 3-0

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Lamine Yamal became the Champions League's top scorer before the age of 20 as Barcelona beat Aston Villa 3-0, taking the mark from Kylian Mbappé. The record comes soon after Mbappé's double decided the Clásico, and the win lifts Barça to second in the league phase.

Lamine Yamal has taken a Champions League scoring record from Kylian Mbappé. He opened the scoring in Barcelona’s win over Aston Villa at Spotify Camp Nou on 3 November. A Villa mistake in the seventh minute gave him the chance. Yamal curled the ball past goalkeeper Zion Suzuki. That goal was his 14th in the competition. No player has scored more before turning 20. Mbappé had managed 13 at that age. The Real Madrid forward now has 72 Champions League goals in total.

The timing gives the record an edge. Mbappé scored in the 41st and 87th minutes as Real Madrid won the season’s first Clásico 2-1 at Spotify Camp Nou on 25 October. Raphinha’s 61st-minute goal was Barcelona’s only reply that night.

Yamal needed 37 Champions League appearances to reach his total. He does not turn 20 until next July. The record could therefore grow further this season. The two forwards had been level at an earlier stage, each with 10 Champions League goals before their 19th birthdays, according to FC Barcelona.

Raphinha did the rest against Villa. He made it 2-0 in the 52nd minute and struck again 12 minutes later. His double moved him alongside Ronaldinho in Barça’s scoring list. It also lifted his tally for the season to 19 goals in 13 games. The club’s match report named him MVP. Goalkeeper Joan García was picked out as the standout player of the first half.

The result matters for the table as much as the record books. Barcelona came into the game after a 4-2 defeat to PSG. A clean sheet and three goals restored some calm.

The win also ended Villa’s perfect start. The English side had won all three of their earlier league-phase games. This was their first Champions League defeat of the season. Aston Villa said it was their first meeting with Barcelona since their two-legged Super Cup win in January 1983.

Barcelona now sit second. Only PSG, with four wins from four, are above them. Villa have the same points total but trail on goal difference. The league phase runs until 27 January 2027.

Harvey AI To Assist CBD Lawyers With Research, Drafting And Review

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Harvey announced on November 6, 2026 that Commercial Bank of Dubai (CBD) had selected its legal AI platform for the bank’s in-house legal team, covering institutional capital markets, investment, regulatory and commercial matters.

The deployment will support legal research, drafting, review and analysis of large document sets, delivered within existing lawyer workflows, including Microsoft Word. The platform will also make CBD’s precedents, policies and prior work product searchable and reusable across the team. Harvey expects that institutional knowledge reuse to help lawyers deliver advice faster and more consistently.

CBD’s lawyers will be able to design their own AI agents for recurring legal workflows, with Harvey’s legal engineers working alongside them. Agent Builder is designed to capture an organization’s expertise and draw on its knowledge, playbooks and preferences, while connectors can provide access to Microsoft products, document-management systems and other tools already in use.

Users can preview an agent’s plan, adjust its scope and approve the work before execution. Harvey says its agents back each claim with a citation and log every step so users can verify accuracy. Those features place approval and verification points around automated work, keeping lawyers involved in decisions about scope and output.

Harvey’s general product examples relevant to CBD’s stated scope include regulatory briefings that synthesize internal policies and trusted sources into a cited action memo, and contract drafting that updates precedent language to reflect a new transaction’s term-sheet requirements. Its in-house documentation separately describes contract review against a preferred playbook, form or template, with drafting suggestions implemented through tracked changes, and large-document analysis in Vault.

CBD evaluated and onboarded the deployment through its third-party risk assessment, with enterprise security and data protection as the baseline. The platform’s enterprise controls include SAML single sign-on, audit logs, IP allow-listing and data lifecycle management. Together, those measures address who can access the system, what activity can be reviewed and how data is handled.

Mikkel Andersen, CBD’s chief risk officer, said responsible adoption of AI is “an important part of building a more agile and resilient bank.” The collaboration is intended to strengthen legal and compliance capabilities, help teams handle increasing regulatory complexity and focus their expertise on strategic priorities. The collaboration is also meant to improve decision-making and support long-term growth, with governance central to the approach.

The bank’s official profile identifies CBD as a UAE-licensed financial institution offering personal and business banking, serving large corporates and providing conventional and Shariah-compliant products. Its stated governance commitments include complying with regulations and standards issued by the UAE Central Bank and the Securities and Commodities Authority. That context gives the deployment’s emphasis on supplier assessment, data protection and lawyer oversight a direct operational purpose.

The deployment makes governance part of everyday legal work rather than only a procurement checkpoint. Its practical value for CBD rests on whether lawyers can reuse more of the bank’s accumulated knowledge while still inspecting sources, adjusting agent plans and approving work before it informs legal decisions.

“We Need to Earn it” Scott Parker’s Simple Message To West Ham Supporters

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

West Ham manager Scott Parker has wasted little time attempting to reconnect the football club with its supporters, and I have to say, I like what I’m hearing. Speaking ahead of Saturday’s Championship fixture against Blackburn Rovers, the new Hammers boss made it clear that the players and coaching staff have a responsibility to earn the backing of the fans.

Much has been made of Parker’s previous association with West Ham, which is understandable given his four excellent years at the club. However, I don’t necessarily believe a manager needs to have played for the Hammers to understand what makes the place special.

I’ve spoken quite a lot recently about small wins and easy wins, and this is another perfect example. Sometimes it doesn’t require an elaborate public relations exercise to make supporters feel appreciated; it simply requires somebody to demonstrate that they understand the football club.

Speaking during his first pre-match press conference, Parker acknowledged that supporters cannot simply be expected to turn up and applaud regardless of what happens on the pitch.

“You need to earn that, and we need to earn that. We can’t expect our fans just to come along and do that.”

He also promised to do everything possible to bring the club together, recognising the importance of the connection between the players and supporters. It’s an encouraging message, particularly after a difficult period in which the relationship between the team and the fanbase has suffered.

I remember when Alan Pardew arrived at West Ham without any previous association with the club. He promised to delve into the Hammers’ history books and learn what West Ham was all about, which struck me as exactly the right approach.

One gesture that has always stuck in my mind was Pardew sending his players out to warm up wearing T-shirts bearing the words “Moore than just a football club”. It was a classy nod towards Bobby Moore and demonstrated that you don’t necessarily need a West Ham playing career to understand the importance of the club’s history.

Noble joined Parker in the West Ham dugout

There was another of those small wins following West Ham’s extraordinary comeback against Cardiff City on Wednesday night. Parker made a point of going over to the travelling supporters and applauding them at the final whistle.

It might seem a relatively insignificant gesture, but I don’t think it is. Parker understands what West Ham means to its supporters, and rather than simply talking about that connection, he’s demonstrating it through his actions.

Scotty P has already spoken enthusiastically about working alongside his former teammate Nobes, highlighting their shared values and the enormous respect Mark Noble commands at the club.

Of course, none of this guarantees success, and ultimately Parker will be judged on whether he can get West Ham promoted. But when approximately 7,000 supporters are preparing to travel to Blackburn, acknowledging their commitment is a pretty good place to start.

I’ve always believed there are certain things a manager can do immediately to improve the atmosphere around a football club. Parker and Noble appear to understand that, and if they can combine those small gestures with the sort of second-half performance we witnessed at Cardiff, they’ll go a long way towards winning supporters over.

Kris Gonzo (commonly known to the West Ham community as Gonzo) is the co-founder of Hammers Chat (established 2014) and the lead video content creator and columnist for Claret and Hugh.

With over a decade of professional sports media experience, he is a frequent West Ham United analyst and contributor for Sky Sports News, BBC Football Focus, and BBC Radio 5 Live. He previously served as an official video and content creator for West Ham United FC during the club's tenure at Upton Park and featured in the official documentary commemorating the historic final game at the Boleyn Ground.

A lifelong Hammer since the legendary 10-0 victory over Bury, when he isn't providing daily match analysis on the Hammers Chat YouTube Channel or broadcasting on X (@GONZObignose), he can be found walking his dog and restoring classic pinball machines.

Opinion: He's the one, South American superstar just showed he's ready to join Liverpool

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Liverpool's next signing should now be obvious.

Andoni Iraola has just entered stormy days at Anfied. But he will walk through it. Given the early signs he has shown in his Liverpool career, there is optimism that a golden light awaits at the end.

Liverpool have always practised patience with their managers. Jurgen Klopp was given plenty of time to rebuild his squad. Even Arne Slot, who seemed to lose all grip and control was given an entire season to rectify his errors.

Iraola will have the courtesy to do the same. Things didn't get off to the perfect start for him at Bournemouth either.

But then in his second season, he delivered the club's best ever finish in the Premier League and subsequently led them to European football for the first time.

Iraola's mission to rebuild Liverpool counts on one manAt Anfield, Iraola has encountered a new challenge. Previously, he has been in charge of underdogs and he has been tasked with winning against the odds. Conversely, in his new role the expectation is already there for Liverpool to be winning matches and major trophies, too. So while not winning his first nine games at Bournemouth counted as a crisis there, losing two games in a row counts as a crisis for Liverpool. Undoubtedly, Iraola's mission to rebuild Liverpool will heavily rely on the club's big money acquisitions. None more so than Florian Wirtz. The Reds have tried to build a team around the German. They have given him runners to lay off passes to like Bradley Barcola and a midfielder who can get the ball to his feet in Trey Nyoni.

Liverpool signed Victor Munoz this summer because Iraola had tracked him during his time at Real Madrid's second team. In that same team was an Argentine playmaker who absolutely dominated opponents in Nico Paz. Now at Como, Paz is starting to dominate on the world stage. This season, he already has three goals and five assists in just four matches in the Champions League. On top of his six goal contributions in nine Serie A matches, that is an incredibly impressive performance from the South American, so far. Paz's latest masterclass in Como's 9-1 win over Lens confirmed that he is ready to join a club like Liverpool, who have been tracking him for a while. The goals that he scored and the way he linked-up with his teammates - it's everything Iraola wants from his no.10. So perhaps it's time for Wirtz to move over and for Paz to replace the misfiring German.

Winter Hiking Gear Checklist for New Hampshire’s White Mountains

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Winter hiking in New Hampshire’s White Mountains requires equipment that can handle rapidly changing conditions. A calm, snowy trail below treeline can become a severe alpine environment within minutes once you reach an exposed ridge or summit.

Temperatures may range from around 40F to –20F, while strong winds, blowing snow, freezing fog, rain, sleet, and glare ice can all occur during the same season. A reliable winter kit must cover both strenuous uphill travel and long periods of inactivity caused by navigation problems, injuries, or changing weather.

This checklist is intended primarily for below-treeline in New Hampshire’s White Mountains, with additional equipment listed for above-treeline terrain such as Franconia Ridge, Mount Washington, the Northern Presidential Range, Mount Lafayette, Mount Carrigain, and Mount Moosilauke.

Winter hikers in New Hampshire may encounter:

Trail conditions can also vary significantly between southern New Hampshire, the central White Mountains, and the higher Presidential Range. Check the forecast for the summit or ridge, not only the trailhead, a common mistake,

The most useful winter clothing system is adjustable. You should be able to remove insulation while climbing and add it quickly when stopping.

Avoid cotton. Wet cotton dries slowly and can accelerate heat loss because your body uses more energy to dry it.

Most New Hampshire day hikers use soft insulated waterproof boots that work with microspikes and snowshoes. Boot insulation is commonly listed as 200 grams or 400 grams. This number describes the insulation thickness, not the boot’s total weight. Mountaineering boots can also work, but they’re stiffer, harder to hike in, and usually limited to the highest peaks when full crampons are required.

Actual warmth depends on boot fit, socks, circulation, pace, wind, and how long you remain stationary. Your toes should move freely. A boot that is too tight can make your feet colder by restricting circulation. Uninsulated leather hiking boots are not recommended: if they get wet, including from perspiration, they freeze.  Uninsulated trail runners don’t provide enough insulation. Basically, you want footwear that will keep your feet warm for the duration of your hike and then some. If a member of your party is injured, you want footwear that will keep your feet warm until search and rescue can arrive. This may take many additional hours.

Avoid oversized pack boots when using microspikes or many types of snowshoes because they provide no ankle support and can be very awkward to hike in. What works for riding a snowmobile or ice fishing won’t work for hiking more than a half of a mile.

Choose wool or synthetic socks, and make sure your boots have enough internal space for them. Sock liners are optional but can reduce friction for hikers who are prone to blisters. Many hikers now tape their heels with Leukotape sports tape instead.

Carry one spare pair in a waterproof bag. If your socks get wet from snow or sweat, changing them can make a big difference.

High gaiters help keep snow out of your boots and add warmth below the knee. They are especially useful on trails with deep snow and loose drifts. They also help protect your lower legs from self-inflicted crampon points when you are tired.

Look for a secure hook-and-loop or snap closure, a durable instep strap, and enough room to fit over your winter pants. Test them with your boots before using them on a New Hampshire mountain.

Carry at least two hats because the one you use during a strenuous climb may get damp.

Wet gloves are common in winter because of perspiration, snow handling, and contact with frozen equipment. Bring multiple pairs rather than relying on one pair for the entire hike.

Mittens are warmer than gloves, while gloves offer better dexterity. A modular system lets you remove the outer shell when your hands begin to sweat. Even better, you can wear a fleece glove inside an uninsulated mitten shell, so you get dexterity when you need it and more warmth when you don’t. Unfortunately, you have to buy them separately, but fleece gloves are relatively inexpensive, and they’re easy to mix and match. Moreover, if you sweat out your fleece gloves, you only need to replace them and continue using the mitten shells, reducing the number of items you need to carry. On long winter hikes, I often carry three pairs of fleece gloves and one pair of mitten shells.

Recommended shell and mitten systems include:

If your route requires an ice axe, choose an insulated glove with enough dexterity to maintain a secure grip. The Black Diamond Punisher Glove is a good option.

Base layers should wick moisture away from the skin. Use wool or synthetic garments rather than cotton.

Thin, moisture-wicking layers often work better for uphill travel than thick garments that trap too much heat.

The mid-layer provides warmth while letting moisture move away from the base layer. Fleece is a dependable choice for New Hampshire’s variable winter conditions.

Options listed from lighter to warmer include:

A fleece hoodie can sometimes serve as an outer layer below treeline when the weather is dry and the wind is light.

A substantial insulated jacket belongs in every winter White Mountains pack, even if you expect to remain active all day. The jacket should be warm enough to wear during a prolonged delay, not merely during a quick break.

Lightweight jackets such as the Mountain Hardwear Ghost Whisperer or Patagonia Nano Puff are generally better suited to three-season use or as supplemental insulation. They may not provide enough warmth as a primary emergency jacket.

A hardshell jacket or rain jacket protects your insulating layers from wind and precipitation. It should be roomy enough to fit over your mid-layer and should include a well-designed hood.

The Foray 3L’s long side zippers provide useful ventilation during steep climbs.

Softshell pants are comfortable and breathable for many dry winter hikes. They are not fully waterproof, however, so carry a shell layer when rain, freezing rain, wet snow, or high winds are possible.

Full-length side zippers are particularly convenient because they allow you to put the pants on without removing winter boots. Test the fit and zipper operation before your hike.

Trail conditions determine which traction system you need. Many hikes require carrying more than one type because conditions may change with elevation.

Microspikes are useful on packed snow and moderate ice. They are light enough to carry on most winter hikes.

For steeper routes with hard ice or more demanding terrain:

These are not a replacement for full mountaineering crampons on technical routes.

Snowshoes provide flotation after storms and on lightly traveled trails. A heel lift is helpful on steep New Hampshire climbs.

Hydration reservoirs and hoses can freeze quickly. Wide-mouth bottles are generally easier to use in cold weather.

Store bottles upside down when possible so ice is less likely to form directly in the cap. Warm water can stay drinkable longer than cold water, and tea or electrolytes may make it easier to stay hydrated.

A 30- to 50-liter pack is usually more practical in winter than a small summer daypack. Extra clothing, food, water, traction, and emergency gear occupy significant space.

Keep sharp traction equipment outside the main compartment or inside a protective bag so it does not damage clothing and emergency insulation.

Winter makes self-sufficiency especially important. Carry:

Use lithium batteries in cold weather because they generally retain performance better than alkaline batteries. Consider carrying two headlamps when daylight is short.

If a hiker becomes injured or immobilized, the group may need to remain in place for several hours. For solo hikes, an unexpected overnight stay can have more serious consequences.

A Therm-a-Rest Z Lite can provide valuable insulation from frozen ground. A 20-degree sleeping bag may be adequate for some day-hike emergencies when combined with all available clothing, but you must consider route, temperature, and personal factors carefully. To melt snow, a dual-fuel stove such as the MSR WhisperLite Universal is a dependable option in very cold weather because it can burn canister gas when temperatures are above 20 degrees or liquid gas when it’s colder.

Exposed New Hampshire summits require more protection than forested trails. Add equipment based on the route, forecast, and group experience.

Goggles can fog, especially when climbing in a hood or face covering. Practice using them before entering exposed terrain.

Use crampons and ice axes only after hands-on training. The Appalachian Mountain Club offers educational programs and guided instruction relevant to northeastern winter travel.

New Hampshire’s winter mountains reward careful preparation, but they leave little room for equipment gaps. A flexible layering system, reliable traction, adequate emergency insulation, and realistic route planning are the foundation of a safer winter day hike.

Philip Werner has hiked twelve rounds of the White Mountain 4000 footers in all twelve months of the year (576 summits), including the months where winter conditions occur: October, November, December, January, February, March, April, and May.

New Mexico Literacy Institute by Multistudio and Design Plus Architects Set to Open in December

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The New Mexico Literacy Institute (NMLI), a 31,000-square-foot educational center designed by Multistudio in collaboration with Albuquerque-based Design Plus Architects, is scheduled to open in December 2026. Located in New Mexico, the project is a legacy initiative of Governor Michelle Lujan Grisham and is intended to provide a statewide facility for literacy education, serving learners of different ages and abilities. The design brings together educational, recreational, and community spaces, approaching literacy as a process that extends beyond conventional classroom settings.

The New Mexico Literacy Institute (NMLI), a 31,000-square-foot educational center designed by Multistudio in collaboration with Albuquerque-based Design Plus Architects, is scheduled to open in December 2026. Located in New Mexico, the project is a legacy initiative of Governor Michelle Lujan Grisham and is intended to provide a statewide facility for literacy education, serving learners of different ages and abilities. The design brings together educational, recreational, and community spaces, approaching literacy as a process that extends beyond conventional classroom settings.

Developed in collaboration with educators experienced in literacy programs and the professionals who will operate the facility, the project incorporates a range of spaces for different learning activities and forms of engagement. Rather than organizing the building around rooms with fixed functions, the design allows environments to accommodate individual study, group activities, teaching, play, and informal interaction. The interior and exterior spaces are designed to host more than 550 learners and educators simultaneously, with visitors able to move between areas according to their needs and interests.

Developed in collaboration with educators experienced in literacy programs and the professionals who will operate the facility, the project incorporates a range of spaces for different learning activities and forms of engagement. Rather than organizing the building around rooms with fixed functions, the design allows environments to accommodate individual study, group activities, teaching, play, and informal interaction. The interior and exterior spaces are designed to host more than 550 learners and educators simultaneously, with visitors able to move between areas according to their needs and interests.

The interiors combine vibrant colors, tactile materials, sculptural forms, and smaller-scale spaces intended to encourage exploration. Books, writing tools, paper, and toys are integrated throughout the building, while built-in bookcases and reading nooks extend learning activities into circulation areas. Staircases and unexpected passageways introduce additional connections between spaces, creating opportunities for visitors to encounter different activities as they move through the building. According to Multistudio Associate Principal Steve Valev, the design aims to provide different settings for learning rather than prescribing a single way of using the building.

Palo Alto Networks in talks to buy AI agent security startup Clutch for $500M

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Palo Alto Networks is in talks to acquire Clutch Security, a startup that secures AI agents’ access to company systems, for $500 million in cash plus additional milestone-based payouts, according to a person familiar with the discussions.

Three-year-old Clutch was last valued at $85 million when it raised a $20 million Series A round in January 2025, led by Lightspeed and SignalFire, according to our source.  The startup is currently approaching $10 million in annual recurring revenue (ARR). That means the acquisition, if completed, would value Clutch at about 50 times its ARR, a standard way of pricing software companies.

Palo Alto Networks and Clutch Security did not respond to a request for comment. The Information was the first to report the acquisition discussions.

The high valuation is not an anomaly in a market increasingly focused on securing software against bad actors using AI to breach systems more easily. Upwind, a startup using AI to secure cloud infrastructure, recently turned down an acquisition offer from DataDog that valued it at roughly 40 times ARR, TechCrunch reported last week.

If the deal happens, it would be Palo Alto Networks’ seventh acquisition this year, according to PitchBook.  The cybersecurity giant has a history of buying innovation rather than building it in-house, and it has ramped up dealmaking this year in response to rising AI-driven cyberattacks.

In September, Palo Alto Networks acquired Console, a two-year-old startup that uses AI agents to automate routine IT help desk tasks. The transaction valued Console at $500 million in stock and cash, TechCrunch reported. Other VC-backed companies that Palo Alto Networks has scooped up this year include NEA-backed Embrace, for $325 million; Chronosphere, an observability platform backed by Greylock and Lux Capital, for $2.95 billion; and Koi, a cyber startup backed by Battery and Team8, for $231 million,  according to regulatory filings.

Several of Clutch’s competitors have also been acquired. Cyera, a cybersecurity startup valued at $12 billion earlier this year, acquired Oasis Security, which, like Clutch, focuses on managing AI agents’ access to systems, for $1 billion. Meanwhile, Cisco bought Menlo Ventures-backed Astrix for roughly $400 million, Calcalist reported.

Playboy's Karissa Shannon Wants Her Son to Be the 'Opposite' of Hugh Hefner

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Karissa Shannon is determined her son will never be like Hugh Hefner.

After sharing shocking details of her time as a Playboy Playmate, the Girls Next Door alum, 37, told Page Six on Thursday, November 5, that she was now engaged and pregnant with a boy.

She told the publication she wanted to use her past experience to teach her son, who she plans to name Genesis, to be “gentleman.”

“I want him to be opposite of whatever Hef was portraying,” Karissa told the outlet. “I want him to be a gentleman, and I want him to have manners, and I want him to lead with his heart.”

Karissa is keeping the identity of her fiancé private but revealed that he is a “simple, blue-collar guy” from Windsor, Ontario and praised him for being so supportive of her past.

“He’s sweet, and he loves me deeply and unconditionally. He’s quick to defend me in comments [on social media] and supports my career and all my projects. He’s proud of who I am,” she said.

Shannon’s comments about her parenting plans for her son come after she detailed eye-opening experiences with Hefner, who died in 2017 at age 91.

“He would try and force me and my sister [Kristina Shannon] to have sex with each other,” Karissa claimed to Page Six in an interview published on Wednesday, November 4. “Me and my sister never actually did it together. So that’s a statement I want to say. Like, really, we never, never had incest[uous] sex. Like, we never did that. We faked it.”

Hefner’s widow, Crystal Harris, previously accused her late husband of “starting to try and shove” Kristina and Karissa’s heads “onto each other’s vaginas to get them to engage with each other sexually.”

“That is incest. That is disgusting,” Harris, 40, claimed on the October 27 episode of the “Lisa Guerrero Unleashed” podcast. “I didn’t want them to have to do something like that with each other. So that was very, very hard.”

Karissa and Kristina both lived in the Playboy estate from the ages of 18 to 20, but they don’t see the experience as a positive one.

Speaking to Us in July 2025, Karissa shared what they would say to the magazine mogul if he was still alive and they had the opportunity.

“I would just ask him why he took advantage of us. We wouldn’t have to have a sit-down. I don’t think there’s an apology that he could issue [to] us that would take back the trauma that he’s done to us,”  Karissa said at the time.

Echoing her sister’s sentiment, Kristina added, “[I would just say], ‘You really did f*** us up and take advantage of us.’ I’d be asking why he thought that was OK.”

Foster bill would bolster tech use to monitor AI financial risks

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A leading Democrat on the House Financial Services Committee introduced legislation Thursday aimed at helping regulators use technology to better spot financial stress turbocharged by artificial intelligence and around-the-clock payment systems.

The draft bill, from Rep. Bill Foster, D-Ill., would require the Federal Reserve to work with other regulators to develop, test and publicly release model software that would improve the ability to assess market conditions in real time and respond quickly to cybersecurity and other threats.

The bill is designed to help regulators keep pace with new risks “driven largely by the development of state-of-the-art AI models,” according to a summary.

The legislation is intended to help regulators provide financing to banks during crises and wind down failing banks. The model software would reduce the amount of manual compliance work that smaller financial institutions would have to do by providing standard data formats for regulatory reporting and tools to manage and transmit the information to regulators, according to a Foster spokesperson.

“With advances in AI creating new cybersecurity threats and financial risks, it’s more important than ever that we modernize the way we monitor the health of our financial institutions,” Foster said in a statement. “We cannot afford to rely on outdated systems to oversee an increasingly complex financial sector.”

Foster, who has a Ph.D. in physics and has been a leader on AI issues on the Financial Services panel, is in line to become chair of the Financial Institutions Subcommittee next year.

He has been mulling a challenge to ranking member Maxine Waters, D-Calif., to chair the full committee in the new Congress. But Foster is now “leaning toward not running,” a person familiar with his thinking said.

Waters already has a confirmed challenger in Rep. Sean Casten, D-Ill., who on Thursday circulated a “Dear Colleague” letter seeking the support of House Democrats for his bid.

Casten touted his “career at the intersection of business, science and public policy.” Before entering Congress, he worked in clean energy and co-founded a company that converted industrial waste energy into usable power.

In his letter, Casten cited the need to lower housing and insurance costs, protect consumers from fraud, address risks posed by cryptocurrency and prediction markets and guard against the next financial crisis. He also promised to hold the Trump administration accountable for ethical lapses.

He’s got a tough road ahead of him, however. Every Financial Services Democrat except Casten, Foster and Rep. Sam Liccardo of California signed an Oct. 21 letter to House Democrats in support of Waters. That show of support came after Casten made it known he was interested and it was clear that Foster had been considering his own bid.

Although Foster may not compete for the chairmanship, he has recently talked to Waters about changing how the committee operates.

“He wants a variety of members to play a role in legislation brought up in committee,” the source familiar with his thinking said. He’s also seeking more input on witnesses who testify at subcommittee hearings.

The draft bill he released on Thursday covers a topic that Foster is likely to pursue throughout the 120th Congress. He often asks the most questions about the use of AI in financial services at hearings and offers the most amendments related to the issue at markups.

Under the bill, model software would be developed for banking records and operations, regulatory reporting, stress testing, anti-money laundering and fraud screening, AI risk monitoring and other activities.

The software must be open source, publicly available and tested for reliability and accuracy, according to the summary. It would be tested using synthetic or legally permitted historical data rather than actual customer accounts. Regulators would not take financial or supervisory actions during the study.

The software developed during the study would not have to be adopted; rather, it “should be viewed as a starting point for further modernization,” the summary says.

All software models would have to be published within one year of enactment of the bill and a final report assessing feasibility, costs and savings, competition and other criteria, would be due to Congress within two years.

The midterm elections’ blue wave could throw a wrench in SpaceX’s mobile ambitions

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Elon Musk has made no secret of his hopes of turning SpaceX into a viable competitor to mobile companies like Verizon and AT&T. But with the Democrats’ resounding victory in the 2026 midterm elections, that plan is suddenly thrown into question.

SpaceX has been busy over the past month making deals and positioning itself to step into the mobile space. In October, it bought an 800 MHz spectrum portfolio from private equity firm Grain Management, which had acquired it from T-Mobile following its 2020 merger with Sprint. And earlier this week, SpaceX struck a deal with Qualcomm to build out base stations for its planned mobile carrier. Taken together, those moves bring the company closer to entering the market.

But to launch the new division, SpaceX will also have to jump through legislative hoops, something that could be significantly more challenging with the Democrats in charge.

Several Democratic candidates made it clear they planned to target Musk—who spent $500 million trying to stop the blue wave—should the party gain a majority in the midterms.

Some lawmakers want to explore potential conflicts of interest in the billions of dollars Musk’s companies receive in federal contracts and subsidies. Others have questions about his possible role in throttling internet access to Ukraine’s army. And several want to do a deep dive into Musk’s role at DOGE (the Department of Government Efficiency that he spearheaded)—which accessed tremendous amounts of sensitive data.

Last December, Democratic Senator Elizabeth Warren of Massachusetts and Democratic Representative Greg Casar of Texas began warning about SpaceX’s possible entry into the mobile market amid a proposed deal to acquire some wireless spectrum licenses from EchoStar.

That deal, they wrote at the time, “would help Musk expand SpaceX’s Starlink capacity and provide an opportunity for the company to gain ‘firepower’ in the satellite communications industry that competitors will struggle to compete with. . . . The close relationship of Mr. Musk to the Trump administration and to key Trump administration officials make it particularly important that the FCC and DOJ engage in a robust, independent, and impartial review.”

The deal, valued at $17 billion, ultimately went through.

Building a network

SpaceX didn’t stop there. Last month’s purchase of the 800 MHz spectrum portfolio seemed mundane at first glance. That frequency was used by Sprint to power its push-to-talk/walkie-talkie-like features in 2004. SpaceX, though, saw it as a way to bypass building an expensive, complex network of repeaters across the U.S. and the globe, when used in conjunction with its Starlink satellites.

“Starlink’s satellite signal at 2 GHz can reach your phone outdoors, but it dies at the first wall,” wrote Aakash Gupta, a product management leader, creator, and educator in a post on X. “800 MHz penetrates buildings. Most phones already have the band built in. So satellites handle the open sky, this low-band layer handles indoors, and suddenly Starlink Mobile covers everywhere a normal carrier does without the tower network.”

Musk was similarly upbeat, writing on X that the portfolio is “the last critical piece of the spectrum puzzle needed for SpaceX to provide complete phone coverage in America.”

The deal with Qualcomm will enhance the cellular infrastructure and enable high-capacity coverage for Starlink Mobile. There are even reports SpaceX is in talks to buy Boost Mobile or use its network, which would give Musk another entry into the mobile market.

Allies and advantages

Come January, Musk will have significantly fewer friends on Capitol Hill—and many more enemies.

“We must get big money out of our politics, so no billionaire or trillionaire can buy our elections,” James Talarico, the Democratic Senator-elect from Texas, said in his victory speech. “We must unrig this economic system that only benefits the top 1%.”

Musk does have one powerful card up his sleeve, however. His relationship with President Donald Trump, which has been rocky in the past, is on solid footing once more, and Trump still controls executive agencies, including the Federal Communications Commission, which could help SpaceX’s entry into the market.

Timing could also play to Musk’s advantage. Verizon’s CEO has estimated it will take at least four years for SpaceX to become a viable player in the market. Musk claimed it would take half that time, but it’s worth remembering he has a history of being overly optimistic in his timeline forecasts.

Whether it’s two years, four years, or more, the past few elections have shown that, politically, a lot can change in that span.
