The Sky This Week from November 6 to 13: Venus in the spotlight

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Friday, November 6The waning Moon leaves us with perfect skies for deep-sky observing. Let’s kick off the week with a bright planetary nebula in Aquarius: NGC 7293, commonly known as the Helix Nebula.

Despite its impressive magnitude of 7.3, the Helix presents a challenge: It is a broad 13’ across, which spreads its light over a relatively large part of sky, lowering its overall surface brightness. That said, you can still find the Helix in large binoculars or any small scope, tucked into southeastern Aquarius, roughly 7.7° southwest of magnitude 3.3 Skat (Delta [δ] Aquarii). If you turn south around 7 P.M. local time, find Skat to the upper right of bright Fomalhaut in Piscis Austrinus, then scan to its lower right, toward 5th-magnitude 66 Aqr. That star lies about halfway between Skat and the Helix Nebula.

Once you’ve located the Helix, the more magnification you can turn on it, the better. Larger amateur scopes will start to show structure in the ring of glowing gas surrounding the central white dwarf, with the northern and southern edges glowing brighter than the eastern and western sides. That gas was once part of the central star, which puffed up and blew away its outer layers as it aged. Sunrise: 6:35 A.M. Sunset: 4:52 P.M. Moonrise: 3:56 A.M. Moonset: 3:16 P.M. Moon Phase: Waning crescent (5%)

Saturday, November 7Venus is our (morning) star of the week, capturing attention above the eastern horizon before sunrise. This morning, the Moon passes 1.1° due south of Earth’s sister planet at 7 A.M. EST.

You can easily catch the pair an hour before sunrise, some 6° high in the east and climbing as the sky begins to brighten. Magnitude –4.5 Venus blazes to the upper left of the delicate crescent Moon, whose western limb is just barely lit as the Sun sets across the lunar nearside. Look for earthshine on the already-shadowed portion of Luna’s face, as sunlight bouncing off Earth lights it up.

Near Venus is another bright light: This is Spica, Virgo’s alpha star, shining at magnitude 1.0 and also lingering in the morning twilight. This morning it lies 1.3° northeast of Venus, with the planet and star roughly side by side above the horizon. Use this view as an anchor point: By the 10th, Venus will have moved to stand due west of this star and will be located to its upper right.

Sunrise: 6:36 A.M. Sunset: 4:51 P.M. Moonrise: 5:01 A.M. Moonset: 3:40 P.M. Moon Phase: Waning crescent (1%)

Sunday, November 8Rising with the stars of western Leo shortly before midnight, bright Jupiter and its Galilean moons put on a show early this morning, better visible the farther east you’re located.

Jupiter is just under 5° northwest (to the upper right) of Regulus as they rise in the east. Fainter Mars stands roughly 3° above Jupiter. You’ll want your telescope trained on the gas giant soon after rising to catch the show, which begins at 1:29 A.M. EST as a shadow appears on the planet’s eastern limb. This is Io’s shadow — but don’t be fooled by the positions of the moons you see in your scope. The moon closest to Jupiter on the east is Callisto, while the moon just east of that is Io, whose shadow we see. Ganymede lies farthest east, while Europa is currently hidden behind the planet in an occultation. The latter appears from behind the eastern limb about 1:54 A.M. EST, not long before Callisto reaches the planet and begins a transit at 2:07 A.M. EST.

Keep an eye on Io and Europa: Io passes north of Europa at 2:23 A.M. EST, then heads into its own transit about 2:42 A.M. EST. Now the race is on, as Io approaches and then catches up with Callisto as the two moons transit, passing Callisto and moving west of it within half an hour. The two moons and Io’s shadow continue to transit until 3:45 A.M. EST, when Io’s shadow leaves the western limb, and Io takes about an hour and a quarter longer before egress. Callisto continues trundling along, finally finishing its transit about 5:51 A.M. CST, with the sky now starting to brighten with twilight in the Midwest.

Sunrise: 6:37 A.M. Sunset: 4:50 P.M. Moonrise: 6:07 A.M. Moonset: 4:08 P.M. Moon Phase: New

Monday, November 9New Moon occurs at 2:02 A.M. EST. Dark skies make tonight the perfect opportunity to try spotting Comet 161P/Hartley-IRAS, still nearly 50° above the western horizon two hours after sunset.

Glowing around 10th magnitude, the comet is currently located in far northern Aquila, near the Eagle’s border with Sagitta the Arrow. You can use Aquila’s bright luminary, Altair, to guide your way — the comet floats 7.7° north-northeast of this star. A closer but fainter guide is 4th-magnitude Alpha (α) Sagittae, just 2.3° north of the comet’s position tonight. Even a small scope will nab you the comet, but larger apertures will yield better views. Under high magnification, look at the comet’s southern side, which should be sharper as it plows into the solar wind.

Not far from Hartley-IRAS’ position is the famous Coathanger asterism, located in Vulpecula the Fox. Cataloged as Collinder 399, you’ll find it roughly 5° northwest of the comet’s position, or about 4° northwest of Alpha Sge. Visible in binoculars or any telescope (in this case, lower magnification is better), you’ll immediately see why this star pattern bears its name. It looks exactly like its namesake, with a line of stars bisected by a small curve extending to the south. At the Coathanger’s eastern edge lies the open cluster NGC 6802.

Sunrise: 6:38 A.M. Sunset: 4:49 P.M. Moonrise: 7:12 A.M. Moonset: 4:40 P.M. Moon Phase: Waxing crescent (1%)

Tuesday, November 10The waxing crescent Moon passes 0.3° south of Antares at midnight EST, although the pair is not visible at that time. You can catch our satellite setting in the west around sunset, although it will likely disappear before Antares pops out of the twilight — that will likely happen only shortly before the star itself sets as well.

Instead, challenge yourself with a different type of observation: picking up Saturn’s moon Iapetus, which tonight reaches greatest eastern elongation 9.4’ east of the ringed planet. The two-toned moon is tidally locked to its parent world, which means it rotates in time with its orbit. At eastern elongation, Iapetus’ darker hemisphere faces earthward, reducing the moon’s magnitude to nearly 12. This, combined with its distance from Saturn, makes it more of a challenge to find, but you can give it a try if you’ve got a larger scope.

Try around 9 P.M. local time when Saturn is highest, some 50° above the southern horizon. It’s the brightest point of light in this region of the sky, hanging just below Pisces and to the far upper left of Fomalhaut, the brightest star in Piscis Austrinus (much closer to the horizon). Even if you can’t locate Iapetus or don’t have a large scope, Saturn is magnificent through a telescope. Its disk spans 19” and its rings are roughly 43” across, showing off their southern side. You should definitely spot its largest and brightest moon, mid-8th-magnitude Titan, just over 2’ west of the planet. Depending on your scope and when you look, you may also spot at least three other moons: 10th-magnitude Tethys, Dione, and Rhea, closer to the planet. Tethys lies to Saturn’s west, while Rhea and Dione are to the east.

Sunrise: 6:39 A.M. Sunset: 4:48 P.M. Moonrise: 8:15 A.M. Moonset: 5:19 P.M. Moon Phase: Waxing crescent (4%)

Wednesday, November 11Venus reaches a stationary point among the stars of Virgo at 10 A.M. EST. This brings its retrograde (westward) motion to an end; after today, Venus will begin moving eastward or prograde through the sky, swinging north of Spica.

You can easily catch the bright planet in the eastern sky before dawn. Venus’ brightness has increased over just the past few days, and it now blazes at magnitude –4.7. An hour before sunrise, you’ll easily spot it alongside Spica, standing just to the star’s upper right. Both are 10° high and rising as dawn approaches. Check out Venus through a telescope: its disk shows off a crescent phase just 10% lit, but stretches an impressive 53” in the sky.

Also just becoming visible at this time is Mercury, barely 1.5° above the horizon and glowing at magnitude 1.2. See if you can locate it about 13° east (to the lower left) of Venus, although you’ll need a particularly flat horizon free of trees, buildings, and lights. Mercury, too, rises as twilight brightens, which may make the planet harder to see in the growing light. Try scanning for it with binoculars, although make sure to put them away well before sunrise from your location, which may differ from the time given below.

Sunrise: 6:41 A.M. Sunset: 4:47 P.M. Moonrise: 9:15 A.M. Moonset: 6:05 P.M. Moon Phase: Waxing crescent (8%)

Thursday, November 12The constellation Perseus is famous for its bright Double Cluster, but these two open clusters aren’t the only groups of young stars in the Hero worth viewing. Today, let’s shift our gaze to the western portion of this constellation, near its border with Andromeda, to check out M34, a mid-5th-magnitude open cluster containing about 100 stars.

Sitting 5.2° west-northwest of 2nd-magnitude Algol (Beta [β] Persei), M34 spans about half a degree, roughly the size of the Full Moon. In physical space, astronomers believe the group spans about 14 light-years and sits 1,400 light-years away. It’s best seen under lower magnifications, such as in binoculars or small amateur scopes (4 inches or so). You’ll spot several dozen stars brighter than 12th magnitude, with its brightest suns spread throughout the cluster.

Sunrise: 6:42 A.M. Sunset: 4:46 P.M. Moonrise: 10:08 A.M. Moonset: 6:57 P.M. Moon Phase: Waxing crescent (14%)

Friday, November 13The Moon reaches apogee, the farthest point from Earth in its orbit, at 12:50 P.M. EST. At that time, our satellite will be 252,040 miles (405,619 kilometers) from Earth.

Celebrate Friday the 13th with something spooky: a ghost in the constellation Andromeda. This evening, point your telescope toward 2nd-magnitude Mirach (Beta [β] Andromedae), already nearly 60° high in the east by 7 P.M. local time. One of the Princess’ brightest stars, Mirach is a cool, reddish M-type giant. It lies some 200 light-years away and shines nearly 2,000 times as brightly as our Sun.

But Mirach is not quite our target — we’re looking for its so-called ghost, just 6.8’ northwest of the star. You’ll need a big scope to see it, but look carefully: Can you spot a faint smudge of light, about 4’ across, close to the star? This is the Ghost of Mirach, a far more distant elliptical galaxy glowing at 10th magnitude and cataloged as NGC 404. It lies some 8 million to 10 million light-years away, just beyond the gravitational bounds of our Local Group of galaxies. It’s called a ghost because it appears almost like a ghostly image of the bright star Mirach, which dominates the view.

Sunrise: 6:43 A.M. Sunset: 4:45 P.M. Moonrise: 10:54 A.M. Moonset: 7:55 P.M. Moon Phase: Waxing crescent (21%)

Alison Klesman is senior editor of Astronomy magazine. She holds a Ph.D. in astronomy and has studied a variety of topics, from minor planets to supermassive black holes.

Nothing but a single joint holds together Dado furniture

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Berlin studio Allen Kaufmann Architekten has adapted a traditional woodworking joint to create a flat-pack range that can be assembled completely without tools – and it’s been voted furniture design of the year by Dezeen readers.

No glue, screws, brackets or other fasteners are used in the Dado tables and shelves.

Instead, their solid-timber construction is held together using only one deceptively simple type of joint: a narrow trench cut across the length of one piece of wood so another can slot into it.

Known as a dado or housing joint, it has been a fixture of woodworking for thousands of years.

But Allen Kaufmann Architekten has reimagined this traditional technique as the only thing holding up an entire self-supporting furniture range that can be easily flat-packed, assembled, demounted and repaired.

“I started with the traditional dado cut, a simple groove cut into timber, and asked whether that basic operation could become an entire structural system rather than simply a joint,” explained studio co-founder Justin Allen.

His patented joint, developed over several years of prototyping, turns dado cuts on their head – or more precisely, at a 45-degree angle – so they can be used to join not just two pieces of wood but four.

This allows a vertical element, a horizontal element and two diagonal braces to lock together at a single point through simple geometry.

“Visually, it has something of a post-and-beam construction, with intersecting diagonal bracing, almost like the inside of a timber barn,” the architect explained. “But the geometry and the way all four elements meet are specific to Dado, giving the joint its own distinct appearance.”

So far, Allen has used this same logic to create a table and a modular shelving system, using a limited set of repeated parts.

As Allen sees it, this offers a more elegant solution to current joinery systems. Furniture with permanent fixings is cumbersome, expensive and emissions-intensive to transport, he argues, while traditional flat-pack systems rely on different screws, brackets and fittings that can be lost or damaged.

In both cases, the joints limit how often and how easily the furniture can be disassembled and reused, which can lead to an otherwise functional piece being abandoned.

With Dado, Allen has avoided the need for fixings and left the joints themselves exposed, in the hope that the furniture’s owner will be able to understand how it works and how it might be maintained.

“The connection remains visible, so the joint becomes the detail, the structure, the assembly and ultimately the design,” he explained.

Each of the range’s standard components is CNC milled with machine precision. This allows the whole range to be produced by a single workshop anywhere in the world – another win in the transport department.

Dado was named furniture design of the year in this year’s Dezeen Awards public vote and is now in the running to be crowned overall winner by a jury of leading industry figures including Norman Foster, Michael Anastassiades and Jaime Hayon.

There, it is up against projects from Tom Fereday, Yabu Pushelberg, String Furniture and more.

Edits launches for MacOS

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Meta’s desktop version of the video creation app supports multiple aspect ratios and was designed with larger screens in mind.

Meta has launched a desktop version of its Edits video editing app, with Mac users now able to download the tool.

The desktop version of Edits gives users a lot more screen space to work with, which should make it easier to utilize its various creation options.

As explained by Brett Westervelt, head of Edits: “We built the desktop editor with creators in mind. It has everything you need to make great videos for any platform, free and customizable by you. Creators get Instagram’s licensed and royalty-free audio libraries, hundreds of sound and video effects, and unlimited automatic captions that support multiple languages.”

The expanded presentation format offers a full multi-track timeline, while having a larger display should help users make more precise edits.

“Splitting, cutting, and trimming are all easier, and color wheels, log conversions, and curves give your videos a finished grade,” Westervelt said. “If you have your own LUTs or font files, you can import them to tailor your Edits to your particular brand and style. For those who use agents in your editing workflows, you can also enable MCP in settings.”

Edits on desktop supports multiple aspect ratios, and offers 4K watermark-free exporting.

Launched in January 2025, Edits was primarily designed to compete with ByteDance’s CapCut app, which offers similar video editing tools but more usage limitations. At the time, there was speculation about ByteDance’s future in the U.S., and Instagram saw the opportunity to launch Edits as a replacement. The tool’s easy integration with Instagram was also positioned as a way to drive more interest in posting short-form content to the app.

As per the Nov. 5 release notes, Edits now has more than 50 million users, and has become a valuable complement for many creators due to its ever-evolving range of customization and creation features.

The desktop version could be its most significant update yet because the bigger layout makes it easier to access and action various options within the creation flow.

Westervelt said that any audio and text styles Edits users have saved on their mobile app will translate to the desktop version when they sign in. The company is also exploring cross-platform editing so users can keep working on projects in either app.

Meta adds new capabilities to Graph and Marketing API

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Businesses that use these tools will be able to add or remove artificial intelligence self-disclosures from posts, although other features have been deprecated.

Meta announced several notable changes to its Graph and Marketing APIs that could impact how businesses manage their Facebook and Instagram presence via third party tools.

Meta’s API access tools enable third-party developers to integrate functions that facilitate Meta profile management, including ad campaigns. For example, these access points enable Facebook ad creation and campaign management via dedicated social media management tools so businesses don’t need to use Meta ad manager tools directly.

It’s worth noting that the majority of Meta advertisers do access the functions via Ads Manager. But API-connected tools are often used by larger agencies and media buyers, enabling more access and cross-platform management within a single tool.

The latest updates from Meta include a range of functional changes that will alter how these third-party tools can facilitate Meta content management, including:

In addition to these updates, which will set new default parameters for manual control, Meta also announced that the total video consumption rate metric is being deprecated. That’s because the 3-second video viewers and video reach metrics were deprecated with Graph API v26.0.

The changes mean that businesses launching Meta ad campaigns via third-party apps will have to update their process to ensure their settings align with their intended process.

TikTok launches standalone shopping app

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The new platform is focused on product discovery and purchasing but is currently only available to a handful of countries in Asia.

TikTok is trying to lure shoppers with a dedicated TikTok Shop app now available for download in select markets in Asia.

As shown in these example screens, the TikTok Shop app is focused primarily on product listings and driving shopping behavior, although some TikTok video content is also accessible.

Alongside personalized product suggestions inspired by user interests, the app also offers select livestreams and short videos to add context to certain product listings.

As explained by TikTok: “Discover trending finds, exclusive deals and shopping support you can count on — all in one place.”

It’s TikTok’s latest effort to enhance shopping behavior, and get more people viewing its platform as a place for product discovery.

That’s been central to the company’s expanded success in China, where the local version of the app, called Douyin, now generates a significant amount of income from in-app sales.

According to KrAsia, Douyin generated nearly $500 billion in gross merchandise volume in 2025, with livestream commerce in particular, driving a significant portion of that activity.

TikTok has been trying to push shopping in its app as well in order to replicate the expanded revenue potential that Douyin has seen from integrating shopping into the video viewing experience.

This play has driven some success, though not on the same scale as in China.

According to Marketing Maze, TikTok globally generated $26.2 billion GMV in the first half of 2025.

Part of the challenge appears to be that TikTok users are seemingly less interested in shopping in-stream, preferring instead to visit separate apps. Shoppers in the U.S., for example, seem more habitually aligned to shopping on Amazon, while they watch videos on TikTok or YouTube. Virtually every social app has tried to merge the two, but none have succeeded in driving significant shopping activity.

Maybe the addition of a separate shopping app could better align with this behavior while still keeping shoppers within the TikTok ecosystem. That could also help to better associate TikTok’s brand with shopping activity and ultimately benefit its broader shopping push.

As reported by social media expert Matt Navarra, the TikTok Shop app is currently available in Singapore, Thailand, Malaysia and the Philippines.

Meta, TikTok and Google push back on California law

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The online giants are arguing that a new law set to take effect next year violates First Amendment rights and oversteps regulatory boundaries, per Reuters.

Social media giants Meta and TikTok have got another major legal fight on their hands as they join Google in seeking to block the enforcement of a new law in California that would force them to stop using “psychologically exploitative features” on users under age 16.

California’s Assembly Bill 1709, which was signed into law by Governor Gavin Newsom on Sept. 10, says that online platforms operating in the state are required to restrict the usage of certain addictive features for users under age 16.

Per the text of the law, those addictive elements include “addictive feeds, endless scrolls, autoplay, and their functional equivalents, including any feature that learns from user information or behavior in order to prolong engagement with a particular internet website, online service, online application, or mobile application.”

Under this new law, social platforms are essentially required to either remove these elements from the accounts of underage users, or ban users under age 16 from using their platforms. The law is currently scheduled to come into effect on Jan. 1.

But as reported by Reuters, platforms are pushing back on these restrictions and arguing that they violate the First Amendment, as well as Section 230 protections for online providers.

As per Reuters: “In separate lawsuits on Wednesday, Google, Meta and TikTok ​said the law wrongly compels them to honor the state’s preferred means of presenting ⁠content to children, and substitutes the state’s judgment for that of parents.”

The argument these platforms are making is that California legislators are overstepping regulatory boundaries in order to impose restrictive operating conditions that infringe on users’ rights.

It’s the latest in a series of significant legal challenges relating to potential teen harm facing social media platforms.

In March, a jury in California found that apps including Facebook, Instagram and YouTube can have significant mental health impacts, particularly on younger users, due to intentionally designed, addictive systems. The jury ruled that both Meta and YouTube parent company Alphabet ignored these risks in order to maximize business opportunities.

Then in August, as part of a separate case, Meta agreed to pay more than $18 billion to halt a potentially damaging lawsuit over social media harms. As part of that settlement, Meta also agreed to implement new restrictions on teen social media use.

These cases have led to the implementation of California’s new law, which the platforms are now seeking to push back on in order to limit the impact of these findings.

Social platforms are also facing various new laws that would ban teens from using their apps entirely in many regions.

Meta makes Muse agents speak

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Let Social Media Today's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

The artificial intelligence-powered tool will be able to recognize conversation queries and respond in a voice selected by the user.

Meta is giving its Muse AI agents speaking capabilities in an effort to make them more engaging and easier to use.

Muse users will be able to select a voice for their personal artificial intelligence-powered agent and empower audio queries.

As explained by Meta: “Sometimes it’s easier to think out loud. With voice, you can talk through an idea with your Muse and let it take shape without worrying about the exact prompt.”

Meta said this should make it easier to use Muse to plan different projects, including work-related prep.

“Some things are better rehearsed out loud,” the company said. “Prepare for a job interview, run through a presentation, or talk through a pitch — hearing a response and answering in the moment makes it feel more like the real thing.”

Meta said the Muse avatar will also be able to express more as it speaks, and will use subtle expressions and gestures that match the tone of the conversation.

“It reacts while you talk, so you can tell it’s following along,” Meta said. “And you choose how present you want it to be: voice only, the avatar alongside your conversation, or full screen when you want to talk face to face.”

Users will even be able to give their Muse characters a custom voice, like giving it a New York accent or making it speak like a pirate.

Which, for some reason, Meta is convinced is something that people want, despite similar options falling flat with users previously.

Back in 2023, Meta introduced celebrity-styled AI chatbots on Messenger, which enabled users to engage with AI bots that could reply, in text form, in the style of various celebrities.

Nobody cared, and less than a year later, Meta shut this feature down due to lack of interest.

But Meta wasn’t done. In August 2024, Meta launched a limited audio version of the same, with the company paying millions of dollars to record the voices of actors such as Judi Dench, Keegan-Michael Key and Awkwafina for AI chatbots.

That didn’t resonate either. Meanwhile, The Wall Street Journal reported in April 2025 that some users had used the feature to generate explicit content voiced by these celebrities. That ultimately led to the demise of the feature before an expanded roll-out.

The addition of custom voices for Muse bots isn’t exactly the same, but even so, Meta seems very dedicated to a novelty idea that nobody else seems to care about.

Either way, users can now give their Muse agent a voice, so that they can walk around in public looking totally normal as they talk out loud to an AI character on their phone.
